\displaystyle\left.+n^{-1}\times\left(\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{3}\kappa_{1}(X_{i})\kappa_{2}(X_{i})\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{5}+\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X_{i})^{3}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{5}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\times\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{3}\kappa_{1}(X_{i})^{2}\kappa_{2}(X_{i})\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{6}\right).

Further, under parts (A4)–(A6) of
Condition A
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{n}^{M}(\zeta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left(n^{-1}\times(\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{4}+\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{5}+\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{6})+n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\times\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{5}+n^{-2}\times\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{6}\right),

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}n^{-1}\left(\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{4}+\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{5}+\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{6}\right),

where the constants
C,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
are independent of
n
. For
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
,
\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}\leq r_{n}\leq\bar{r}_{n}=O\{\sqrt{\log(n)}\}
, so the displayed bound is
O\{n^{-1}\log(n)^{3}\}
and, in particular, is bounded by
C\log(n)^{6}/n
after enlarging
C
. This proves the desired result.
∎

S2.7.6
Step 5: expanding
\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}

Recalling (
S.15a
), we define
F_{n}(\zeta)
from the expansion of
M_{n}
as

\displaystyle M_{n}(\zeta)=\underbrace{\frac{1}{4}\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}\zeta+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta)}_{\triangleq F_{n}(\zeta)}+\varepsilon_{n}^{M}(\zeta).

(S.22)

Consider now the modification of (
S.12
) obtained by replacing
M_{n}
with
F_{n}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}.

The main result of this step is the following asymptotic expansion of the above quantity.

Proposition S2.18

(Expansion of
F_{n}^{*}
)
.

Under Assumption 2.5 and
Condition A
, if (
S.12
) holds, then

\displaystyle\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\rangle+\varepsilon^{F}_{n},

(S.23)

where
\mathcal{V}_{n},~\xi_{n}
, and
\mathcal{K}_{n}
are defined immediately after (6) in
Theorem 3.1, and

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{F}_{n}\right|\leq C\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n},

where
C
is a constant independent of
n
.

The proof of Proposition
S2.18
follows these steps:

1.

We first present a preparatory result that bounds
L_{n}(\zeta)
in Lemma
S2.19
.

2.

We show in Lemma
S2.20
that, with high probability, the optimizer on the left-hand side of (
S.23
) is attained in
\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
.

3.

When the optimizer is in
\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
, we compute the asymptotic expansion of the optimizer in Lemma
S2.21
, where Lemma
S2.19
is applied.

4.

Combining these steps leads to the complete proof.

Lemma S2.19

(Bounding
L_{n}(\zeta)
)
.

Under Assumption 2.5 and
Condition A
, the following bounds hold for any
\zeta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
:

\displaystyle|L_{n}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{3}}{8}\left(1+\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right]\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{2}}{8}\left(1+\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right]\right).

Proof of Lemma
S2.19
.

Recall that by (
S.15b
),
L_{n}
is a cubic form of
\zeta
:

\displaystyle L_{n}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta,\gamma,\omega\in[d_{h}]}\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\omega}(X_{i})^{\top})\zeta^{(\beta)}\zeta^{(\gamma)}\zeta^{(\omega)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\left(\sum_{\beta\in[d_{h}]}\zeta^{(\beta)}\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}(X_{i})\right)\Sigma\left(\sum_{\gamma\in[d_{h}]}\zeta^{(\gamma)}\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}(X_{i})\right)\Sigma\left(\sum_{\omega\in[d_{h}]}\zeta^{(\omega)}\mathcal{D}h^{\omega}(X_{i})^{\top}\right)\right).

Then, by Assumption 2.5, we get

\displaystyle|L_{n}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{3}\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X_{i}).

Thus, by part (A4)(i) of
Condition A
, we get

\displaystyle|L_{n}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{3}}{8}\left(1+\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right]\right),

which is the first inequality in the statement of the lemma.

As for the gradient
\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta)
, we have, for
\eta^{\top}=(\eta^{(1)},\ldots,\eta^{(d_{h})})
,

\displaystyle\eta^{\top}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta,\gamma,\omega\in[d_{h}]}\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\omega}(X_{i})^{\top})\eta^{(\beta)}\zeta^{(\gamma)}\zeta^{(\omega)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\beta,\gamma,\omega\in[d_{h}]}\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\omega}(X_{i})^{\top})\zeta^{(\beta)}\eta^{(\gamma)}\zeta^{(\omega)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\beta,\gamma,\omega\in[d_{h}]}\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\omega}(X_{i})^{\top})\zeta^{(\beta)}\zeta^{(\gamma)}\eta^{(\omega)}.

Then by Assumption 2.5, we get

\displaystyle|\eta^{\top}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{3}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\eta\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X_{i}),

thus, by part (A4)(i) of
Condition A
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X_{i})\leq\frac{3\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{2}}{8}\left(1+\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right]\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S2.20

(Tail bound of the solution in
F_{n}^{*}(-H_{n})
)
.

Under Assumption 2.5 and
Condition A
, define
\mathcal{Z}_{n}
as in (
S.11
) and define
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}
as an optimizer:

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\arg\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}.

If (
S.12
) holds, then
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
.

Proof of Lemma
S2.20
.

This proof follows the same pattern as the proof of
Proposition
S2.13
.
For
\zeta
satisfying
\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}=r_{n}
, Lemma
S2.19
and
Condition A
(A4)(i) give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}}\left(-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|H_{n}\right\|_{2}-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{n}r_{n}+\frac{B_{\Sigma}r_{n}^{2}}{8\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle s_{n}-2s_{n}+\frac{8B_{\Sigma}s_{n}^{2}}{\lambda_{n}^{2}\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-s_{n}\left(1-\frac{8B_{\Sigma}s_{n}}{\lambda_{n}^{2}\sqrt{n}}\right)<0,

where the last inequality follows from (
S.12
) and (A1)–(A2). Thus
-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)<0
on the boundary. Note that for any optimizer
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}
, we have

\displaystyle-\zeta_{n}^{\dagger\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})\geq-\mathbf{0}^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\mathbf{0})=0.

Therefore,
\left\|\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}<r_{n}
, implying that
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
.
∎

Lemma S2.21

(Expansion of the solution in
F_{n}^{*}(-H_{n})
)
.

Under Assumption 2.5 and
Condition A
, let
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}
be the
optimizer in Lemma
S2.20
. If

\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
, then

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}=-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})+\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger},

(S.24)

with

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{5}{2}}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{4}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),

where
\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\cdot)
is the gradient function of the function
L_{n}(\cdot)
defined in (
S.15b
), and
C
is a constant independent of the sample size
n
.

Proof of Lemma
S2.21
.

First, note that
F_{n}(\cdot)
is continuously differentiable with respect to its argument because
F_{n}
is a third-order polynomial. When
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
,
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}
satisfies the first-order optimality condition:

\displaystyle-H_{n}-\mathcal{D}F_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})=\mathbf{0}.

By the definition of
F_{n}(\cdot)
in (
S.22
), we have

\displaystyle-H_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{V}_{n}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})=\mathbf{0}\quad\implies\quad\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{V}_{n}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=-H_{n}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}).

By part (A1) of
Condition A
,
\lambda_{\min}(\mathcal{V}_{n})>0
, so
\mathcal{V}_{n}
is invertible. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}\left(-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})\right)

\displaystyle=-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}\left(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})\right).

In the last equality above, we have exploited that
L_{n}
is a cubic form; hence,
\mathcal{D}L_{n}
is a quadratic form.

By part (A2) of
Condition A
, we have
\left\|H_{n}\right\|_{2}\leq a_{H}\sqrt{\log(n)}
, and by part (A1)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathds{E}\left[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}\right]\right)^{-1}\triangleq 2\sigma_{\min}^{-1}.

By Lemma
S2.19
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{8}\left(1+\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right]\right).

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}\left(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})\right)-\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}\left(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\left\|\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|H_{n}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\left\|\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{4}}{n}\right)=C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{5}{2}}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{4}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),

where
C
and
C^{\prime}
are constants independent of
n
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
S2.18
.

Proof of Proposition
S2.18
.

By Lemma
S2.20
, when (
S.12
) holds, we have
\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
and
\|\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}\|_{2}<r_{n}\leq\bar{r}_{n}=O\{\sqrt{\log(n)}\}
. We have

\displaystyle\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=-(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})=\underbrace{-(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})^{\top}H_{n}}_{(D)}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{4}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}}_{(E)}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})}_{(F)},

where the second equality follows from the definition of
F_{n}(\cdot)
in
(
S.22
). Next, we substitute the expansion (
S.24
)
from Lemma
S2.21
and compute each term, noting that
part (A2) of
Condition A
gives

\left\|H_{n}\right\|_{2}\leq a_{H}\sqrt{\log(n)}
.

•

For part (D), by plugging in (
S.24
), we get

\displaystyle-(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})^{\top}H_{n}=

\displaystyle 2H_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}+\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}H_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})-H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\rangle+\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}}\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\rangle-H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}.

By Lemma
S2.21
, we have an upper bound of
\left\|\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}
. Thus

\displaystyle\left|H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}\right|\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{9}{2}}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),

where
C_{1}
is a constant independent of the sample size
n
.

•

For part (E), by plugging in (
S.24
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}\zeta_{n}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}+\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}H_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})-H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}+\frac{16}{n}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}+\frac{1}{4}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\rangle+\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}}\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\rangle-H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}+\frac{16}{n}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}+\frac{1}{4}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}.

By Lemma
S2.19
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{16}{n}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})\right|\leq C_{2}n^{-1}\log(n)^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|\leq C_{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(n)\left\|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right\|_{2}.

•

For part (F), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}\left(-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}-\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)\qquad\text{by~\eqref{eq:zetan_dagger}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}\left(-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}\right)=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\rangle\qquad\text{by $L_{n}$: \eqref{eq:expanL}, $\mathcal{K}_{n}$: Theorem~3.1}.

By Lemma
S2.19
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|-\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{4}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(n)+\left\|\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}\right)\triangleq b_{n}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}\left(-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}\right)\right|\leq C_{5}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\log(n)^{2}b_{n}+\log(n)b_{n}^{2}+b_{n}^{3}\right).

Combining these inequalities gives

\displaystyle\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\rangle+\varepsilon^{F}_{n},

and we have

\displaystyle|\varepsilon^{F}_{n}|\leq

\displaystyle 2\left|H_{n}^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|+\left|\frac{16}{n}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})\right|+\left|\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\mathcal{D}L_{n}(-\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n})^{\top}\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|+\frac{1}{4}\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}\right\|_{2}\left\|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\dagger})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}\left(-2\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}H_{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{6}\left(\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{9}{2}}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\log(n)^{2}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{9}{2}}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)+\left(\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n}+\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{9}{2}}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\log(n)^{2}b_{n}+\log(n)b_{n}^{2}+b_{n}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n},

where all
C,C_{1},\ldots,C_{6}
are constants independent of
n
. This completes the proof.
∎

S2.7.7
Combining steps

Combining the previous five steps completes the proof of the asymptotic expansion in Theorem 3.1.

Proof of Theorem 3.1.

Applying Proposition
S2.5
, under Assumptions 2.4 and 2.5, we have

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}}\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-M_{n}(\zeta)\}.

(S.25)

Applying Proposition
S2.13
, under Assumptions 2.4 and 2.5, there is a deterministic integer
N_{1}
, such that when
n\geq N_{1}
, (
S.12
) holds, and thus

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:step5supMn}=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-M_{n}(\zeta)\}.

(S.26)

Applying Proposition
S2.16
, if Assumptions 2.5, 2.6 and
Condition A
hold, we obtain

(
S.26
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)-\varepsilon^{M}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\right\}+\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{n},

(S.27)

where for
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
,

\displaystyle F_{n}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{4n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})^{\top}\zeta+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}L_{n}(\zeta),

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{n}^{M}(\zeta)\right|\leq C\frac{\log(n)^{6}}{n},

and
C
is a constant independent of
n
. As a result,
|\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{n}|\leq C\frac{\log(n)^{6}}{n}
.

Applying Proposition
S2.18
, under
Condition A
and the same condition (
S.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:step5supMn3}=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\varepsilon^{F}_{n}+\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{n},

(S.28)

where the error term
\varepsilon^{F}_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{F}_{n}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\frac{\log(n)^{3}}{n},

where
C^{\prime}
is a constant independent of
n
. As a result, we obtain (6).

Finally, Proposition
S2.12
gives a deterministic integer
N_{2}

such that, when
n\geq N_{2}
,
Condition A
holds with probability at least

1-O(n^{-1})
. Taking
N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
concludes the proof.
∎

S2.8
Other proofs in Section 3

S2.8.1
Signed-root reduction for Theorem 3.2

Recall that

V=\mathds{E}[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}],\qquad W=\mathds{E}[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}],

and define

\tilde{\xi}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{-1}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})
. The expansion in
Theorem 3.1 can be rewritten as

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left\langle V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle}_{(A)}+\widetilde{O}_{p}(n^{-1})=\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}+\widetilde{O}_{p}(n^{-1}).

(S.29)

Here
(A)
is expressed as the squared norm of an asymptotically normal vector

\gamma=(\gamma_{j})_{j\in[d_{h}]}\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
, commonly called the signed-root
statistic; see, e.g.,
[
30
, Section 3.2]
. Specifically, for
every
\eta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\gamma,\eta\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle V^{\frac{1}{2}},\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\eta\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes(V^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes(V^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta)\right\rangle.

(S.30)

Writing

m_{k}^{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{k}}=\mathds{E}[\gamma_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\gamma_{\alpha_{k}}]
, its first three moments satisfy

\displaystyle m_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{1,n}^{j}}{\sqrt{n}},

\displaystyle m_{2}^{jk}

\displaystyle=\left(V^{-\frac{1}{2}}WV^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)_{jk}+O(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle m_{3}^{jk\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{3,n}^{jk\ell}}{\sqrt{n}},

(S.31)

for
j,k,\ell\in[d_{h}]
, where
\mu_{1,n}^{j}
and

\mu_{3,n}^{jk\ell}
are bounded deterministic coefficients.
Appendix
S2.13
gives a deterministic expectation
representation for these coefficients.

Applying the multivariate delta method and the Edgeworth expansion

[
12
, Theorem 2(b)]
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z}\tilde{\phi}(v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z}\left(\sum_{j\in[d_{h}]}\mu_{1,n}^{j}\mathrm{h}_{1}^{j}(v)+\sum_{j,k,\ell\in[d_{h}]}\frac{\mu_{3,n}^{jk\ell}}{6}\mathrm{h}_{3}^{jk\ell}(v)\right)\tilde{\phi}(v)\mathrm{d}v}_{(B)=0}+O(n^{-1}),

(S.32)

where
\tilde{\phi}
is the density of
V^{-\frac{1}{2}}H
for
H\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},W)
,
and
\mathrm{h}_{1}^{j}
and
\mathrm{h}_{3}^{jk\ell}
are the first- and third-order
multivariate Hermite polynomials; see (
S.110
). Both polynomials
are odd, whereas the integration region and
\tilde{\phi}
are centrally symmetric.
Consequently,
(B)=0
, so the null CDF error is of order
n^{-1}
rather than

n^{-1/2}
.

S2.8.2
Proof of Theorem 3.2

Lemma S2.22

(Tail bounds of average III)
.

Under Assumptions 2.7, 2.9, and 2.10, let
Y_{VK}
be the centered joint
collection of one-observation summands that generate

\mathcal{V}_{n}-V
and
\mathcal{K}_{n}-\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}]
, represented in an orthonormal
basis of its covariance support after deterministic linear redundancies
have been removed. Fix any constant

a_{VK}>\sqrt{2\lambda_{\max}\{\mathrm{Cov}(Y_{VK},Y_{VK})\}}
. With probability
at least
1-o(n^{-1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\|_{F}\leq a_{VK}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n}},\quad\left\|\mathcal{K}_{n}-\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}]\right\|_{F}\leq a_{VK}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n}}.

(S.33a)

As a consequence, there exists a deterministic sequence
\bar{\delta}_{n}=\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
, such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle=\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n},

(S.33b)

with
\gamma
defined by (
S.30
) and
|\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}|\leq\bar{\delta}_{n}
, where
\mathcal{V}_{n},\mathcal{K}_{n}
are defined immediately after (6) in Theorem 3.1, and
V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}
are defined in the discussion preceding (
S.29
).

Proof of Lemma
S2.22
.

In this proof, the constants
C_{1},\ldots,C_{9}
are independent of the sample size
n
.

In the covariance-support representation just defined,

\mathrm{Cov}(Y_{VK},Y_{VK})
is positive definite, and the Euclidean norm of

Y_{VK}
agrees with the norm of the full centered collection. Moreover,
Assumptions 2.9 and 2.10 give
Y_{VK}
a finite fourth moment.
The Cramér regularity in Assumption 2.7 supplies the required nonlattice
condition. Hence these empirical averages admit joint Edgeworth expansions
up to order
n^{-1}
.

Applying Lemma
S2.9
to
Y_{VK}
shows that, with probability at least
1-o(n^{-1})
, (
S.33a
) holds, and hence

\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\|_{2}\leq a_{VK}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n}}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}-V^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n}},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}-\left(V^{-1}-V^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)V^{-1}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq C_{2}\frac{\log(n)}{n}.

To prove the second claim, recall that
\tilde{\xi}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{-1}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})
. By Proposition
S2.12
,

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq a_{H}\sqrt{\log(n)},

and thus

\left\|\xi_{n}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{3}\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle-\left\langle V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1},\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right)^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle-\left\langle V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}-\left(V^{-1}-V^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)V^{-1}\right),\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right)^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\right|\leq C_{4}\frac{\log(n)^{2}}{n},

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n}-\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n}-\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|+\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n}-\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}-\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{5}\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{7}{2}}}{n}.

Therefore, we obtain

\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle=\left\langle V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n},

with
|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}|\leq C_{6}\log(n)^{\frac{7}{2}}n^{-1}
. Finally, note that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle\right|\leq C_{7}\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle\right|\leq C_{8}\frac{\log(n)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{n}},

(S.34)

we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle=\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}^{(0)},

for the vector
\gamma
defined by (
S.30
), where

|\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}^{(0)}|=O\{\log(n)^{3}n^{-1}\}
. Combining this relation
with the preceding display and absorbing
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}^{(0)}

into
\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}
gives (
S.33b
), with

|\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}|\leq C_{9}\log(n)^{7/2}n^{-1}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem 3.2.

By Theorem 3.1 and Lemma
S2.22
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-1})
,

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}=\underbrace{\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{\text{(A')}}+\underbrace{\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}}_{\text{(B')}},

where
\gamma
is defined in (
S.30
), and
|\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}|\leq\delta_{n}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}
of order
\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
.

Now consider the Edgeworth expansion of part (A’). By Assumption 2.9, we have
\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{4}\right]<\infty
and
\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{8}\right]<\infty
. The Cramér regularity in Assumption 2.7 therefore permits an Edgeworth expansion for
\gamma
with error of order
n^{-1}
. Thus, (
S.32
) is valid.

To deal with part (B’), write
Q_{n}=\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}
and

r_{n}=\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}
, and let
\mathcal{E}_{n}
be the event on which

nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})=Q_{n}+r_{n}
and
|r_{n}|\leq\delta_{n}
. The preceding probability
bound gives
\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\mathcal{E}_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-1})
, and, for every
z
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}(Q_{n}\leq z-\delta_{n})-\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\mathcal{E}_{n}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}^{\star}(nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq z)\leq\mathbb{P}^{\star}(Q_{n}\leq z+\delta_{n})+\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\mathcal{E}_{n}^{c}).

(S.35)

Fix
z_{0}>0
. For all sufficiently large
n
,
\delta_{n}\leq z_{0}/2
.
The distribution
\Psi
has a continuous density on
(0,\infty)
that is
bounded on
[z_{0}/2,\infty)
; hence, for some constant
C_{z_{0}}
,

\sup_{z\geq z_{0}}|\Psi(z\pm\delta_{n})-\Psi(z)|\leq C_{z_{0}}\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}(n^{-1}).

Combining this bound, (
S.35
), and the expansion
(
S.32
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{z\geq z_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\star}(nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq z)-\Psi(z)\right|=\widetilde{O}(n^{-1}),

(S.36)

which completes the proof.
∎

S2.8.3
Proof of Theorem 3.3

The stochastic expansion under local alternatives uses the following uniform
version of the null regularity conditions.

Assumption S2.23

(Uniform regularity under local alternatives)
.

Assume that

(i)

(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star})_{n\geq 1}
is contained in a class
\mathcal{P}_{c}
that is
compact in total variation, and every element of
\mathcal{P}_{c}
satisfies
Assumption 2.7.

(ii)

\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=O(n^{-1/2})
and

\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}]\in\mathbb{S}_{++}
for every
n
. Moreover, for some

\sigma_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}]\right)\wedge\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}]\right)\geq\sigma_{0}\qquad\forall n\geq 1.

(iii)

The required moments are bounded uniformly:

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathds{E}_{n}\!\left[\left(\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}+\kappa_{1}(X)+\kappa_{2}(X)\right)^{8}\right]<\infty.

The compactness condition yields the uniform Cramér bound used below. For
example, total-variation convergence follows from Scheffé’s lemma

[
49
]
when the laws have densities
f_{n}
converging almost
everywhere to a density
f_{\infty}
. The eigenvalue bounds prevent
\xi_{n}
and

\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1}
from becoming unstable, while the uniform moments control the
remainder independently of
n
. These conditions hold, for instance, when

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
and the relevant moments converge to a null law satisfying
Assumptions 2.8 and 2.9, with

\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=O(n^{-1/2})
.

Lemma S2.24

(Uniform Cramér condition)
.

Assume that
Y_{n}
and
Y_{\infty}
have laws
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
, respectively, and that every
Y_{n}
and
Y_{\infty}
satisfies the Cramér condition. If
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
converges to
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
in total variation as
n\to\infty
, then
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star})_{n\geq 1}
satisfies the uniform Cramér condition: for every
b>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t:\left\|t\right\|_{2}\geq b}\left|\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}t^{\top}Y_{n}\right)\right]\right|<1.

Proof of Lemma
S2.24
.

In the following, we use
\mathds{E}_{n}
and
\mathds{E}_{\infty}
to denote the expectation under
\mathbb{P}^{\star}_{n}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
, respectively. We prove this by contradiction.
Suppose that there is a sequence of
(n_{k})_{k\geq 1}
and
b_{0}>0
, such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t:\left\|t\right\|_{2}\geq b_{0}}\left|\mathds{E}_{n_{k}}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}t^{\top}Y_{n_{k}}\right)\right]\right|=1.

Because
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
converges to
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\star}
in total variation, uniformly over
t
,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left|\mathds{E}_{n_{k}}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}t^{\top}Y_{n_{k}}\right)\right]-\mathds{E}_{\infty}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}t^{\top}Y_{\infty}\right)\right]\right|\leq\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\text{TV}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n_{k}}^{\star},\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\star}\right)=0,

where
\text{TV}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
denotes the total variation distance between
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
. Consequently,

\sup_{t:\left\|t\right\|_{2}\geq b_{0}}\left|\mathds{E}_{\infty}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}t^{\top}Y_{\infty}\right)\right]\right|=1
,
contradicting the Cramér condition for
Y_{\infty}
under
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\star}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem 3.3.

Note that the proof of Theorem 3.1 relies only on Assumptions 2.4–2.6 once

Condition A
is available. Proposition
S2.12
establishes
that condition with probability
1-O(n^{-1})
under the null. Therefore, in
the setting of Theorem 3.3, it remains to establish the same probability
bound under Assumption
S2.23
.

Items 2 and 3 of Assumption
S2.23
provide the uniform
moment bounds needed to repeat the argument establishing

Condition A
in Proposition
S2.12
, except for
part (A2).

For part (A2), we establish an Edgeworth expansion for

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{h}(X_{i})-\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)])

under the local alternative
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
:

•

Let
X(n)
have law
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
. By item 1 of Assumption
S2.23
,
\mathbf{h}(X(n))
satisfies the Cramér condition under every
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
. Since
(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star})_{n\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{P}_{c}
and
\mathcal{P}_{c}
is compact in total variation, a subsequence argument using Lemma
S2.24
gives the required uniform Cramér condition.

•

By item 2 of Assumption
S2.23
and

\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=O(n^{-1/2})
, for all sufficiently large
n
,

\lambda_{\min}\!\left\{\mathrm{Cov}_{n}(\mathbf{h}(X),\mathbf{h}(X))\right\}\geq\lambda_{\min}\!\left\{\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}]\right\}-\left\|\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)]\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2}.

•

By item 3 of Assumption
S2.23
,
\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathds{E}_{n}{\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{4}}<\infty
, and

\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{4};\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}\geq\varepsilon n^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}n^{2}}\sup_{m\geq 1}\mathds{E}_{m}\left[\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{8}\right]\longrightarrow 0.

Therefore,
[
5
, Theorem 20.6]
implies that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{h}(X_{i})-\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathbf{h}(X)])
admits an Edgeworth expansion under the local alternative
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
. Letting
\Lambda_{H,\mathrm{loc}}=\sup_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{\max}\{\mathrm{Cov}_{m}(\mathbf{h}(X),\mathbf{h}(X))\}<\infty
and fixing any
a_{H,\mathrm{loc}}>\sqrt{2\Lambda_{H,\mathrm{loc}}}
, Lemma
S2.9
gives, with probability at least
1-o(n^{-1})
,

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{h}(X_{i})-\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)\right]\right)\right\|_{2}\leq a_{H,\mathrm{loc}}\sqrt{\log(n)}.

By item 2 of Assumption
S2.23
,
\left\|\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)\right]\right\|_{2}=O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Thus, we get

\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq(a_{H,\mathrm{loc}}+1)\sqrt{\log(n)},

when
n
is sufficiently large. The change of constant in front of
\sqrt{\log(n)}
does not affect the proof of Theorem 3.1. Therefore, we obtain the desired result by following the same proof of Theorem 3.1.
∎

S2.9
Proofs in Section 3.3

Lemma S2.25

(Gradient of
z_{1-\varrho}
)
.

Let
z_{W,V}=F_{W,V}^{-1}(1-\varrho)
for
W,V\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}\times d_{h}}
, where

\displaystyle F_{W,V}(z)\triangleq\int_{\left\{v\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}:~v^{\top}W^{\frac{1}{2}}V^{-1}W^{\frac{1}{2}}v\leq z\right\}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v,

then, for
W,V\in\mathbb{S}_{++}
, the function
z_{W,V}
is twice continuously differentiable with respect to
(W,V)
, and

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}}{z_{W,V}}=\frac{\left\langle L_{W,V},\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle}{\left\langle L_{W,V},V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle}\triangleq\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathrm{d}W,\mathrm{d}V),

(S.37)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\mathrm{d}V\right)e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s-V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}\left(\mathrm{d}W\right)W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}+\int_{0}^{\infty}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\mathrm{d}V\right)e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle L_{W,V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z_{W,V}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d_{h}}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}v\right)\sqrt{\det\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\left(-\frac{1}{2}vv^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}V^{-\frac{1}{2}}WV^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v.

(S.38)

Proof of Lemma
S2.25
.

Under the change of variables
v\leftarrow z^{-\frac{1}{2}}V^{-\frac{1}{2}}W^{\frac{1}{2}}v
, we can write
F_{W,V}
as

\displaystyle F_{W,V}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d_{h}}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{z}{2}v^{\top}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}v\right)\sqrt{\det\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}z^{d_{h}/2}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\triangleq\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1}f_{W,V,z}(v)\mathrm{d}v.

On compact subsets of
\mathbb{S}_{++}\times\mathbb{S}_{++}\times(0,\infty)
,

f_{W,V,z}(v)
is twice continuously differentiable with respect to

(W,V,z)
, and its derivatives through order two are bounded by an
integrable envelope. Applying
[
25
, Theorem 2.27]
twice
shows that
F_{W,V}(z)
is twice continuously differentiable with respect
to
(W,V,z)
. Further,
F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z)\neq 0
for
z>0
.

By definition,
F_{W,V}(z_{W,V})-(1-\varrho)=0
. The implicit function
theorem
[
40
, Theorem 3.2.1]
therefore shows that

z_{W,V}
is twice continuously differentiable with respect to
(W,V)
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{d_{h}\times d_{h}}

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}}{\mathrm{d}W}+\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{W,V,z_{W,V}}(v)}{\mathrm{d}W}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{d_{h}\times d_{h}}

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}}{\mathrm{d}V}+\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{W,V,z_{W,V}}(v)}{\mathrm{d}V}\mathrm{d}v,

where
\mathbf{0}_{d_{h}\times d_{h}}
is the
d_{h}\times d_{h}
zero matrix. As a result, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})}\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z_{W,V}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d_{h}}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}v\right)\sqrt{\det\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\left\langle-\frac{1}{2}vv^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}V^{-\frac{1}{2}}WV^{-\frac{1}{2}},\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})}\left\langle L_{W,V},\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\rangle,

where
L_{W,V}
is defined in (
S.38
) and we apply the differentiation equations:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}Xv\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}Xv\right)\left\langle-\frac{1}{2}vv^{\top},\mathrm{d}X\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\sqrt{\det X}=\sqrt{\det X}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}X^{-\top},\mathrm{d}X\right\rangle.

As for
F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})
, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{W,V}(z_{W,V})=

\displaystyle\int_{\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z_{W,V}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d_{h}}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}v\right)\sqrt{\det\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{2z_{W,V}}v^{\top}V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}v+\frac{d_{h}}{2z_{W,V}}\right)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z_{W,V}}\left\langle L_{W,V},V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\rangle.

As for
\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathrm{d}V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)W^{-1}V^{\frac{1}{2}}+V^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}W^{-1}\right)V^{\frac{1}{2}}+V^{\frac{1}{2}}W^{-1}\mathrm{d}\left(V^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

where
\mathrm{d}W^{-1}
and
\mathrm{d}V^{\frac{1}{2}}
are

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}W^{-1}=-W^{-1}\left(\mathrm{d}W\right)W^{-1},\qquad\mathrm{d}V^{\frac{1}{2}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\mathrm{d}V\right)e^{-sV^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{d}s.

The first identity follows from the Neumann series, while the second follows
from
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
for a symmetric infinitesimal matrix

\mathrm{d}V
. It is straightforward to verify that
\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}/\mathrm{d}W

and
\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}/\mathrm{d}V
are continuously differentiable in
(W,V)
;
for the second-order derivative of
V^{\frac{1}{2}}
, see

[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
. This completes the proof.
∎

S2.9.1
Quantile expansion for the size theorem

Proposition S2.26

(Second-order expansion of
\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}
)
.

Under Assumptions 2.7–2.9, there is a deterministic sequence
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-1}\right)
and an integer
N
such that, when
n\geq N
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=z_{1-\varrho}\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}\left(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right)\right)+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n},

where
\mathbb{P}^{\star}(|\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}|\leq\tilde{\delta}_{n})=1-O(n^{-1})
,
W=\mathds{E}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}\right],V=\mathds{E}\left[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}\right]
, and
\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the linear functional defined in (
S.37
).

Proof of Proposition
S2.26
.

Assumption 2.9 gives finite eighth moments for both
\mathbf{h}(X)
and

\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)
. Let
Y_{WV}
be the centered joint collection of
one-observation summands that generate
\mathcal{W}_{n}-W
and
\mathcal{V}_{n}-V
,
represented in an orthonormal basis of its covariance support after
deterministic linear redundancies have been removed, and fix any

a_{WV}>\sqrt{2\lambda_{\max}\{\mathrm{Cov}(Y_{WV},Y_{WV})\}}
. Assumption 2.7
supplies the Cramér condition, so Lemma
S2.9
yields the
following bounds with probability at least
1-o(n^{-1})
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{h}(X_{i})^{\otimes 2}-\mathds{E}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}\right]\right)\right\|_{F}\leq a_{WV}\sqrt{\log(n)},\\
&\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})^{\top}-\mathds{E}\left[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}\right]\right)\right\|_{F}\leq a_{WV}\sqrt{\log(n)}.\end{split}

(S.39)

Consequently,
\left\|\mathcal{W}_{n}-W\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\|_{2}
are of order
\sqrt{\log(n)/n}
, where
W=\mathds{E}[\mathbf{h}(X)^{\otimes 2}]
and
V=\mathds{E}[\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)^{\top}]
. On this event,
\mathcal{W}_{n}
and
\mathcal{V}_{n}
are invertible for all sufficiently large
n
, and hence
\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}
is well-defined. By Lemma
S2.25
, for
\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=F_{\mathcal{W}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n}}^{-1}(1-\varrho)
and
z_{1-\varrho}=F_{W,V}^{-1}(1-\varrho)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}-z_{1-\varrho}\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}-z_{1-\varrho}-\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}}{\mathrm{d}W},\mathcal{W}_{n}-W\right\rangle-\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{W,V}}{\mathrm{d}V},\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\left\|\mathcal{W}_{n}-W\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2Ca_{WV}^{2}\log(n)n^{-1},

where
\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
S.37
), and
C
is a constant independent of
n
.
∎

S2.9.2
Proof of Theorem 3.4

Proof of Theorem 3.4.

This proof follows the pattern of the proof of Theorem 3.2. With probability
at least
1-O(n^{-1})
under
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
, we have

nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})=\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}.

Also, by Proposition
S2.26
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-1})
,

\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=z_{1-\varrho}\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}.

Here, we have

\displaystyle|\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}|+|\varepsilon_{n}|+|\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}|\leq\delta_{n}

(S.40)

for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\left(\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}.

(S.41)

Let
a_{n}=\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)
and define

\tilde{\gamma}=(1+a_{n}/2)\gamma
. Since
a_{n}=\widetilde{O}_{p}(n^{-1/2})
,

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=(1-a_{n})\left(\left\|\tilde{\gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}_{p}(n^{-1}).

(S.42)

The remaining argument makes this reduction uniform on the required high-probability event
and applies the Edgeworth expansion to the corresponding signed root.

Under Assumption 2.9,
\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{8}\right]<\infty,\mathds{E}\left[\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{8}\right]<\infty
. Using Lemma
S2.9
, by the same reasoning as in the proof of Proposition
S2.26
, we have
\left\|\mathcal{W}_{n}-W\right\|_{2},\left\|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right\|_{2}
are of order
\sqrt{\log(n)}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with probability at least
1-o(n^{-1})
.

Therefore, there are constants
C_{1},C_{2}
independent of
n
, such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n}},

(S.43)

and thus

\left|\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\right|\leq C_{2}\frac{\log(n)}{n}.

Let
\Gamma_{\gamma}=V^{-\frac{1}{2}}WV^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and fix any

a_{\gamma}>\sqrt{2\lambda_{\max}(\Gamma_{\gamma})}
. Since
\gamma
has an
Edgeworth expansion up to order
n^{-1}
(see the proof of Theorem 3.2), the
argument of Lemma
S2.9
gives, with probability at least

1-o(n^{-1})
,
\left\|\gamma\right\|_{2}\leq a_{\gamma}\sqrt{\log(n)}
.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:nRn_expand1}=

\displaystyle\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\left(\left\|\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}\right)

\displaystyle+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\left(\left\|\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n},

(S.44)

with the newly added error
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}
satisfying
|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}|\leq C_{3}\log(n)^{3}n^{-1}
for a constant
C_{3}
.

We further expand the term
\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\gamma
to remove the higher order terms (higher than
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
). Concretely, there is a vector
\gamma^{\dagger}
defined by

\displaystyle\langle\gamma^{\dagger},\eta\rangle=

\displaystyle\left\langle V^{\frac{1}{2}},\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\eta\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\left\langle V^{\frac{1}{2}},\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\eta\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V,\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\left(V^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\tilde{\xi}_{n}\otimes\left(V^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\right)\right\rangle\hskip 8.19447pt\forall\eta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}.

(S.45)

Further, by (
S.34
) and (
S.43
),

\displaystyle\left\|\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\gamma\right\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n},~~|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}|\leq C_{4}\frac{\log(n)^{4}}{n}.

(S.46)

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:nRnexpand2}=\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}.

(S.47)

When
n
is sufficiently large,
|\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)|\leq\frac{1}{2}
; thus
1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)>0
.

Finally, following the delta method again as in (
S.35
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq 0\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq 0\right)

by (
S.47
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)\right)\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)\leq 0\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

by (
S.40
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\leq 0\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

|\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\cdot,\cdot)|\leq\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}\leq 0\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:eps4}}.

The remaining steps and the reverse inequality are similar to those in (
S.35
).

For the Edgeworth expansion of
\gamma^{\dagger}
, note that its moment expansion has the same form as that of
\gamma
in (
S.31
). Assumption 2.7 supplies the required Cramér condition, and the moment conditions above validate the expansion. Consequently,

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=\Psi(z_{1-\varrho})+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)=1-\varrho+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Therefore, the proof is complete.
∎

S2.9.3
Proof of Theorem 3.5

The second-order power expansion requires the following strengthening of
Assumption
S2.23
.

Assumption S2.27

(Additional Edgeworth regularity)
.

Assume that Assumption
S2.23
holds and, in addition,

(i)

for some
\delta^{\prime}>0
,

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathds{E}_{n}\!\left[\left(\left\|\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}\right)^{8+\delta^{\prime}}\right]<\infty,\qquad\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathds{E}_{n}\!\left[\left(\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\kappa_{1}(X)\right)^{4+\delta^{\prime}}\right]<\infty;

(ii)

there is a limiting law
\mathbb{P}^{\star}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}
such that

(a)

\mathbb{P}^{\star}
satisfies Assumptions 2.7–2.10;

(b)

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
converges to
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
in total variation.

The first condition validates an order-
n^{-1}
Edgeworth expansion for the
joint fluctuations of
\mathcal{W}_{n}
,
\mathcal{V}_{n}
, and
\mathcal{K}_{n}
; the formulation in

[
11
, Assumption (c)]
takes
\delta^{\prime}=1
. We do not require
their moments to possess expansions in powers of
n^{-1/2}
, so the correction
coefficients may depend explicitly on
n
. The scalar location-shift case in
Proposition
S2.28
gives those coefficients explicitly.

For each
i\in[n]
, define
Y_{i}\triangleq f(X_{i})
by

\displaystyle Y_{i}=\big(

\displaystyle(h^{\alpha})_{\alpha\in[d_{h}]},(h^{\beta}h^{\gamma})_{1\leq\beta\leq\gamma\leq d_{h}},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\gamma\top})_{1\leq\beta\leq\gamma\leq d_{h}},(\mathcal{D}h^{\beta}\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h^{\gamma}\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\omega\top})_{1\leq\beta\leq\omega\leq d_{h},\ \gamma\in[d_{h}]}\big)(X_{i}).

For each
n
, the vectors
(Y_{i})_{i\in[n]}
are i.i.d. under
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
; we
write
Y
for a generic copy.

Proof of Theorem 3.5.

First, Assumption
S2.27
and Lemma
S2.24
validate the Edgeworth expansion of

\left(\sqrt{n}\left(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W_{n}\right),\sqrt{n}\left(\mathcal{V}_{n}-V_{n}\right)\right).

Proposition
S2.26
therefore continues to hold under the local alternatives
\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\right)_{n\geq 1}
.

Next, following the same steps as in the proof of Theorem 3.4, it remains to show a valid Edgeworth expansion of
\gamma^{\dagger}
(
S.45
). Under local alternatives
\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\right)_{n\geq 1}
, the definition of
\gamma^{\dagger}
is different. We therefore introduce
\gamma^{\ddagger}
, defined by

\displaystyle\langle\gamma^{\ddagger},\eta\rangle=

\displaystyle\left\langle V_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}},\bar{\xi}_{n}\otimes\eta\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{W_{n},V_{n}}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n}-V_{n})\left\langle V_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}},\bar{\xi}_{n}\otimes\eta\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n}-V_{n},\bar{\xi}_{n}\otimes\left(V_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\right)\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathcal{K}_{n}],\bar{\xi}_{n}\otimes\bar{\xi}_{n}\otimes\left(V_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\right)\right\rangle\hskip 8.19447pt\forall\eta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}},

where

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})=\sqrt{n}V_{n}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})-\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)\right]\right)+\sqrt{n}V_{n}^{-1}\mathds{E}_{n}\left[\mathbf{h}(X)\right].

Lemma
S2.24
and

[
4
, Theorem 2(b)]
imply that

\gamma^{\ddagger}
admits an Edgeworth expansion up to order
n^{-1}
.

Finally, the computation of the explicit Edgeworth-expansion formula is deferred to Lemma
S4.1
in Supplementary Section
S4
.
∎

S2.9.4
Proof of Proposition
S2.28

Regularity for location shifts

With
\theta_{n}=\tau_{0}/\sqrt{n}
, assume that
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\star}
has a density
f_{0}

on
\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
satisfying
f_{0}(x-\theta_{n})\to f_{0}(x)
for almost every
x
.
The model in Assumption 3.7 is equivalently

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}(S)=\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\star}(S-\theta_{n}),\qquad S\in\mathcal{B}(\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}),

where
S-\theta_{n}=\{x:x+\theta_{n}\in S\}
. Thus
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
has density

f_{n}(x)=f_{0}(x-\theta_{n})
, and Scheffé’s lemma gives

\left\|f_{n}-f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}}\to 0
.

Proposition S2.28

(Power expansion of WP-based test under location shift)
.

Suppose Assumption 3.7 and the conditions of
Theorem 3.5 hold with
d_{h}=1
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)>\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v}_{\text{first-order}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}E_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})}_{\text{second-order}}+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

(S.48)

where
\phi
is the density of
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,

\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
is the
(1-\varrho)
-quantile of the chi-square
distribution with one degree of freedom, and

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}},

\displaystyle E_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\left\{k_{1}v+\frac{k_{2}}{2}(v^{2}-1)+\frac{k_{3}}{6}(v^{3}-3v)\right\}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v.

The coefficients are

\displaystyle k_{1}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{2\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}-\frac{\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}]\mathds{E}_{0}[h(X)\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}{2\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}}{2\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}]\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0},

(S.49a)

\displaystyle k_{2}={}

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{2}^{2}}+\frac{2\tilde{\alpha}_{3}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0},

(S.49b)

\displaystyle k_{3}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}+\frac{3\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}.

(S.49c)

Here

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}_{0}[h(X)^{j}],\quad j=2,3,

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}],

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}],

with
\alpha_{2},\tilde{\alpha}_{2}>0
.

Notation.
In this section, we set

\displaystyle\alpha_{j,n}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}}[h(X)^{j}],

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{2,n}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}],

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{3,n}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathds{E}_{n}[\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}],

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})-\alpha_{1,n},

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle\triangleq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})^{2}-\alpha_{2,n},

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{2}

\displaystyle\triangleq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})^{\top}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2,n},

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{3}

\displaystyle\triangleq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})^{\top}-\tilde{\alpha}_{3,n}.

Proof of Proposition
S2.28
.

Using Theorem 3.3 and the identity, valid when
d_{h}=1
,

\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{\mathcal{W}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}

for
\mathcal{W}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n}\in\mathds{R}
, we obtain the asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle nR_{n}(h)-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{\mathcal{W}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\left(\bar{\gamma}^{2}-\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\varepsilon_{n},

where
|\varepsilon_{n}|\leq\delta_{n}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-1}\right)
,
\bar{\gamma}=\sqrt{n}\left(R_{1}+R_{2}\right)
for
R_{j}=O_{p}(n^{-j/2}),

j=1,2
. Specifically,

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2,n}}}(A_{1}+\alpha_{1,n}),

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{2,n}^{\frac{3}{2}}}A_{2}(A_{1}+\alpha_{1,n})+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3,n}}{2\sqrt{\alpha_{2,n}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2,n}^{2}}(A_{1}+\alpha_{1,n})^{2}.

Then, using Theorem 3.5,
nR_{n}(h)-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}
has a valid Edgeworth expansion whose coefficients depend on the cumulant expansion of
\bar{\gamma}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)>\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}q(v)\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the density function of
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and

\displaystyle\tau=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathds{E}_{0}\left[\mathcal{D}h(X)\right]\tau_{0}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}\in\mathds{R},

\displaystyle q(x)=

\displaystyle k_{1}x+\frac{k_{2}}{2}\left(x^{2}-1\right)+\frac{k_{3}}{6}\left(x^{3}-3x\right).

The constants
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
are the first-order coefficients in the
expansions of the first three cumulants of
\bar{\gamma}
. Their
derivation is deferred to Supplementary Section
S4.2
.
∎

S2.9.5
Proof of Proposition 3.8

The next lemma supplies the triangular-array EL expansion used in the
comparison. It is stated separately because applying a fixed-law expansion
pointwise for each
n
would not by itself control its remainder along the
sequence of transformed laws.

Lemma S2.29

(Uniform scalar EL expansion under location shifts)
.

Suppose Assumption 3.7 and the conditions of Theorem 3.5 hold with
d_{h}=1
,
and let
Z_{n,i}=h(X_{i})
for
X_{i}\sim\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}^{EL}(h)>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)={}

\displaystyle\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}F_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})+o(n^{-1/2}),

(S.51)

uniformly along the location-shift sequence, where

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}},

\displaystyle F_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\left\{\tilde{k}_{1}v+\frac{\tilde{k}_{2}}{2}(v^{2}-1)\right\}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v,

and

\displaystyle\tilde{k}_{1}={}

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{2\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}-\frac{\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}]\mathds{E}_{0}[h(X)\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}}{\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{6\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha_{3}}{3\alpha_{2}^{5/2}}\tau_{0}^{\top}\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}]\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0},

(S.52a)

\displaystyle\tilde{k}_{2}={}

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{2}^{2}}+\frac{4\alpha_{3}}{3\alpha_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}.

(S.52b)

Proof.

Write
\widehat{m}_{r,n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{n,i}^{r}
and

\bar{Z}_{n}=\widehat{m}_{1,n}
. Because
\mathds{E}_{0}[Z_{0}]=0
and

\alpha_{2}>0
, both
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\star}(Z_{0}<0)
and
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\star}(Z_{0}>0)
are
positive. Total-variation convergence of
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}
to
\mathbb{P}_{0}^{\star}

therefore gives a constant
p>0
such that, for all sufficiently large
n
,
each of these two probabilities is at least
p
. Consequently,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}\left(0\notin(\min_{i\leq n}Z_{n,i},\max_{i\leq n}Z_{n,i})\right)\leq 2(1-p)^{n}=o(n^{-1/2}).

Thus the ordinary scalar EL multiplier exists with probability

1-o(n^{-1/2})
.

On this event, let
\lambda_{n}
solve

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{Z_{n,i}}{1+\lambda_{n}Z_{n,i}}=0.

The uniform moments and nondegeneracy in the conditions of Theorem 3.5 give

\bar{Z}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\widehat{m}_{2,n}^{-1}=O_{p}(1),\qquad\widehat{m}_{r,n}=O_{p}(1),\quad r=3,4,

where all stochastic orders here and below are uniform along the array. They
also imply
\max_{i\leq n}|Z_{n,i}|=o_{p}(\sqrt{n})
. Expanding the multiplier
equation uniformly on
|\lambda_{n}|\max_{i}|Z_{n,i}|=o_{p}(1)
gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}=\frac{\bar{Z}_{n}}{\widehat{m}_{2,n}}+\frac{\widehat{m}_{3,n}\bar{Z}_{n}^{2}}{\widehat{m}_{2,n}^{3}}+O_{p}(n^{-3/2}).

Substitution into the scalar EL dual yields

\displaystyle nR_{n}^{EL}(h)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log(1+\lambda_{n}Z_{n,i})

\displaystyle=\frac{n\bar{Z}_{n}^{2}}{\widehat{m}_{2,n}}+\frac{2n\widehat{m}_{3,n}\bar{Z}_{n}^{3}}{3\widehat{m}_{2,n}^{3}}+O_{p}(n^{-1}).

(S.53)

Since
\widehat{m}_{3,n}-\alpha_{3,n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, this can be written as

\displaystyle nR_{n}^{EL}(h)=\Gamma_{n,EL}^{2}+r_{n},\qquad\Gamma_{n,EL}=\frac{\sqrt{n}\bar{Z}_{n}}{\sqrt{\widehat{m}_{2,n}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}\alpha_{3,n}\bar{Z}_{n}^{2}}{3\widehat{m}_{2,n}^{5/2}}.

(S.54)

The truncation and empirical-moment bounds used in the proof of Theorem 3.5strengthen the displayed stochastic remainders as follows: for a deterministic

\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
,

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}(|r_{n}|\leq\delta_{n})=1-o(n^{-1/2}).

(S.55)

Indeed, on the same truncation event the Taylor remainder in the multiplier
equation is bounded by a polynomial in the uniformly bounded empirical
moments times
n^{-3/2}
, and the omitted fourth-order term in the EL dual is

\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
. The complement has probability
o(n^{-1/2})
by
Assumption
S2.27
.

It remains to justify the distributional expansion uniformly in
n
. The
vector
(Z_{n,i},Z_{n,i}^{2})
is a subvector of the empirical-moment vector
used in the proof of Theorem 3.5. Hence the uniform moment and Cramér
conditions in Assumption
S2.27
and
Lemma
S2.24
apply; if this vector has a redundant coordinate, we
use the maximal nondegenerate subvector reduction described in
Section
S2.6
. The triangular-array Edgeworth theorem
used in the proof of Theorem 3.3, followed by the smooth-transformation
argument of
[
4
, Theorem 2(b)]
, therefore applies
to (
S.54
). The transformation is uniformly smooth
on a neighborhood of the population moments because

\inf_{n}\mathds{E}_{n}[Z_{n}^{2}]>0
.

To make the coefficient calculation explicit, set

U_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\bar{Z}_{n}-\alpha_{1,n})
,

T_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\widehat{m}_{2,n}-\alpha_{2,n})
, and

a_{n}=\sqrt{n}\alpha_{1,n}
. A second-order Taylor expansion of
(
S.54
) gives

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n,EL}={}

\displaystyle\frac{a_{n}+U_{n}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{2,n}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{-\frac{(a_{n}+U_{n})T_{n}}{2\alpha_{2,n}^{3/2}}+\frac{\alpha_{3,n}(a_{n}+U_{n})^{2}}{3\alpha_{2,n}^{5/2}}\right\}+O_{p}(n^{-1}).

Here

\operatorname{Cov}_{n}(U_{n},T_{n})=\alpha_{3,n}-\alpha_{1,n}\alpha_{2,n}

and

\kappa_{3,n}(U_{n})=n^{-1/2}(\alpha_{3,n}-3\alpha_{1,n}\alpha_{2,n}+2\alpha_{1,n}^{3})
.
Combining these identities with the translated-moment expansions in
(
S.113
) gives the first three cumulants of

\Gamma_{n,EL}-\tau
as

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}(\Gamma_{n,EL}-\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{k}_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}+O(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}(\Gamma_{n,EL}-\tau)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\tilde{k}_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+O(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}(\Gamma_{n,EL}-\tau)

\displaystyle=O(n^{-1}),

with
\tilde{k}_{1},\tilde{k}_{2}
in (
S.52
). In particular, the
order-
n^{-1/2}
skewness term cancels in the EL signed root. In CDF form,
the resulting uniform Edgeworth expansion is

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathds{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\star}(\Gamma_{n,EL}-\tau\leq x)-\int_{-\infty}^{x}\left[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\{\tilde{k}_{1}v+\frac{\tilde{k}_{2}}{2}(v^{2}-1)\right\}\right]\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v\right|=o(n^{-1/2}).

Finally, (
S.55
) and the preceding expansion imply
that replacing
nR_{n}^{EL}(h)
by
\Gamma_{n,EL}^{2}
changes the rejection
probability by at most
o(n^{-1/2})+\widetilde{O}(n^{-1})
: off the exceptional
event, a disagreement can occur only within
\delta_{n}
of either fixed
boundary. Integrating over the two rejection tails therefore proves
(
S.51
). The coefficients coincide with the scalar
mean formula in
[
14
, Theorem 3.1]
, which provides an
independent fixed-law check of the calculation.
∎

Proof of Proposition 3.8.

Since the power expansion of Hotelling’s
T^{2}
test is a special instance of
Proposition
S2.28
obtained by setting
\tilde{\alpha}_{3}=0
, it
remains to derive the EL comparison. Set
Z_{n,i}=h(X_{i})
. The EL constraint
depends on
X_{i}
only through
Z_{n,i}
, and hence

R_{n}^{EL}(h;X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})=R_{n}^{EL}(\operatorname{id};Z_{n,1},\ldots,Z_{n,n}).

Lemma
S2.29
gives its uniform triangular-array power
expansion and the coefficients in (
S.52
).

Comparing the power expansion of the WP-based test in Proposition
S2.28
with that of the EL test, we examine the difference in the
n^{-1/2}
term. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})-F_{2}(\varrho,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}-\frac{2\alpha_{3}}{3\alpha_{2}^{2}}\right)I_{2}(\varrho,\tau),

where
\tau=\mathds{E}_{0}[\mathcal{D}h(X)]\tau_{0}/\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}
is the scalar standardized
drift from Proposition
S2.28
.
For
w_{1}\triangleq\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}-\tau
and

w_{2}\triangleq\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}+\tau
, define

I_{2}:(0,1)\times\mathds{R}\to\mathds{R}
by

\displaystyle I_{2}(\varrho,\tau)

\displaystyle\triangleq\int_{|v+\tau|^{2}>\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}\left(\frac{1+\tau^{2}}{2}v+\tau(v^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{2}(v^{3}-3v)\right)\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v

(S.56)

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}{2}(\phi(w_{1})-\phi(w_{2})).

The power gap between the WP test and Hotelling’s
T^{2}

test has the same formula as above, with
0
in place of

2\alpha_{3}/(3\alpha_{2}^{2})
.
∎

S2.9.6
Proof sketch of Theorem 3.10

The fourth-order tensor in Theorem 3.10 is

\resizebox{22157625}{}{$\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{n}\triangleq\left(-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{\omega^{1}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\omega^{2}}_{\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}h^{\omega^{3}}_{\beta}h^{\omega^{4}}_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{3n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{\omega^{1}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}h^{\omega^{2}}_{\beta}h^{\omega^{3}}_{\beta^{\prime}}h^{\omega^{4}}_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}+S_{\gamma\omega^{1}\omega^{2}}(\mathcal{V}_{n}^{-1})_{\gamma\theta}S_{\theta\omega^{3}\omega^{4}}\right)_{\omega^{1},\omega^{2},\omega^{3},\omega^{4}\in[d_{h}]}$}.

Here all summands are evaluated at
X_{i}
, and

\displaystyle A_{\gamma\omega^{1}\omega^{2}}

\displaystyle\triangleq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{\gamma}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\omega^{1}}_{\beta}h^{\omega^{2}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}},

\displaystyle S_{\gamma\omega^{1}\omega^{2}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2}A_{\gamma\omega^{1}\omega^{2}}+A_{\omega^{1}\gamma\omega^{2}}.

The tensor
A
is symmetric in its last two indices. Thus
S
is precisely the
coefficient obtained by differentiating the cubic form: if

P_{3,n}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{8}A_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\zeta^{(\alpha)}\zeta^{(\beta)}\zeta^{(\gamma)}
, then

\left\{\mathcal{D}P_{3,n}(-2\xi)\right\}_{\gamma}=S_{\gamma\omega^{1}\omega^{2}}\xi^{(\omega^{1})}\xi^{(\omega^{2})}.

Repeated indices are summed using the Einstein convention. Only the full
symmetrization of
\mathcal{L}_{n}
contributes to

\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle
.

Example S2.30

(Quadratic moment function II)
.

Suppose
\mathbf{h}(x)=\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2}-1
,
\Sigma=I_{d_{X}}
,

\mathds{E}[\left\|X\right\|_{2}^{2}]=1
, and the support of
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
is compact. The
projection problem (4) has the exact expansion

nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})=n\left(\sqrt{1+\frac{\Delta_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}}-1\right)^{2}=n\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}}{2\sqrt{n}}-\frac{\Delta_{n}^{2}}{8n}+\frac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{16n^{3/2}}+O_{p}(n^{-2})\right)^{2},

(S.57)

where
\Delta_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}-1)
. Theorem 3.10instead gives

\text{RHS of~(12)}=\frac{\Delta_{n}^{2}}{4}-\frac{\Delta_{n}^{3}}{8\sqrt{n}}+\frac{5\Delta_{n}^{4}}{64n}+O_{p}(n^{-3/2}),

(S.58)

and the two expressions agree through order
n^{-1}
.

Remark S2.31

(Proof adjustments for Theorem 3.10)
.

This proof follows the steps used for Theorem 3.1 and described in Section
S2.7.1
. Specifically, Steps 2, 4, and 5 require the following adjustments:

(1)

For
Step 2
, Assumption 3.9 includes the global
Jacobian-Lipschitz condition of Assumption 2.5 and gives sub-exponential
tails for its envelope
\kappa_{1}(X)
, for
\mathbf{h}(X)
, and for the local
derivatives up to order four. By choosing the constants in the logarithmic
envelopes sufficiently large, a union bound for the sub-exponential tails
gives an event of probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-3/2}\right)
on which these
quantities are bounded by deterministic logarithmic envelopes. On this event, the tail
bounds (A3)–(A6) in

Condition A
of Proposition
S2.12
follow with corresponding
logarithmic bounds, which are absorbed by the
\widetilde{O}(\cdot)
notation.
The checks of (A1) and (A2) follow from the same concentration arguments,
using Lemma
S2.9
and Lemma
S2.10
.

(2)

Additionally, on the same high-probability event, the third-order
derivatives of
\mathbf{h}
and the envelopes
\kappa_{1}(\cdot),\kappa_{2}(\cdot)

are bounded by deterministic polylogarithmic quantities; here one may take

\kappa_{2}(x)=\sup_{\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}\leq 1}\left\|\mathcal{D}^{3}\mathbf{h}(u)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
These factors are again absorbed into the
\widetilde{O}(\cdot)
remainders.

(3)

As for
Step 4
, we will instead show (in the sequel) that

M_{n}(\zeta)=F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)+\varepsilon^{M\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\quad\forall\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}

for a quartic form
F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)
. Above,
\mathcal{Z}_{n}
is defined as in (
S.11
).

(4)

As for
Step 5
, we will instead provide the expansion: with high probability,

\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right).

With the above adjustments, the final step will be the same as stated in Section
S2.7.7
with
F_{n}^{\dagger}
(see Proposition
S2.32
) in place of
F_{n}
.

We now prove items (3) and (4) in the following two sections.

S2.9.7
Proof of Theorem 3.10 – part I

This section proves item (3) listed in Section
S2.9.6
. We fix a
vector
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
and introduce the following notation.

Notation.
In this section only, let

•

\Delta_{i}=\left(\Delta^{\alpha}_{i},\alpha\in[d_{X}]\right)\triangleq\Delta_{n,i}(\zeta)
for every
i\in[n]
.

•

\bar{h}\triangleq\zeta^{\top}\mathbf{h},~~\left(\bar{h}_{\beta},\beta\in[d_{X}]\right)\triangleq\mathcal{D}(\zeta^{\top}\mathbf{h}),~~\left(\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma},\beta,\gamma\in[d_{X}]\right)\triangleq\mathcal{D}^{2}(\zeta^{\top}\mathbf{h})
.

We formalize the result in the following proposition.

Proposition S2.32

(Expanding
M_{n}(\cdot)
, cf. Proposition
S2.16
)
.

Under Assumption 3.9, there exists a deterministic
integer
N
such that, on the logarithmic-envelope event of
Remark
S2.31
and whenever
Condition A
holds, for every

n\geq N
,

\displaystyle M_{n}(\zeta)=F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)+\varepsilon^{M^{\dagger}}_{n}(\zeta),

where
\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}|\varepsilon^{M^{\dagger}}_{n}(\zeta)|\leq\delta_{n}^{\dagger}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}^{\dagger}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
, and

\displaystyle F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\underbrace{\frac{1}{4}\bar{h}_{\alpha}\bar{h}_{\beta}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}}_{\text{quadratic term}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bar{h}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\beta}\bar{h}_{\beta^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}}{8}}_{\text{cubic term}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\frac{\bar{h}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\beta}\bar{h}_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}}{16}}_{\text{quartic term}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\frac{\bar{h}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\bar{h}_{\beta}\bar{h}_{\beta^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}}{48}}_{\text{quartic term}}\right)(X_{i}).

To prove this proposition, we need the following expansion of
\Delta_{i}
for
i\in[n]
.

Lemma S2.33

(Expanding
\Delta_{i}
, cf. Lemma
S2.15
)
.

Under Assumption 3.9, there exists a deterministic
integer
N
such that, on the logarithmic-envelope event of
Remark
S2.31
and whenever
Condition A
holds, the
following expansion holds uniformly for every
n\geq N
,
i\in[n]
,

\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
, and
\alpha\in[d_{X}]
:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\alpha}_{i}=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{2}\bar{h}_{\beta}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma}\bar{h}_{\omega}\Sigma_{\omega\gamma}}{4}+\frac{1}{n}\frac{2\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma}\bar{h}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega\gamma}\Sigma_{\gamma^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}}+\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma\omega}\bar{h}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\gamma^{\prime}\gamma}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\omega}}{16}\right)(X_{i})

\displaystyle+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
S2.33
.

Using Lemma
S2.15
, equation (
S.14b
) reads

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}(\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h})^{\beta}\left(X_{i}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{i}\right).

(S.60)

On the logarithmic-envelope event in Remark
S2.31
, we have

\max_{i\leq n}\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq C\log(n)

for a constant
C
. Then by equation (
S.14c
) and
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}\leq C\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}\log(n)^{2}.

As a result, we apply the Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(\cdot)
around
X_{i}
in (
S.60
) and get

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\left(\bar{h}_{\beta}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma}\Delta_{i}^{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2n}\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma\omega}\Delta_{i}^{\gamma}\Delta_{i}^{\omega}\right)(X_{i})+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{2}\bar{h}_{\beta}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma}\bar{h}_{\omega}\Sigma_{\omega\gamma}}{4}+\frac{1}{n}\frac{2\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma}\bar{h}_{\omega\omega^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega\gamma}\Sigma_{\gamma^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}}+\bar{h}_{\beta\gamma\omega}\bar{h}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\bar{h}_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\gamma^{\prime}\gamma}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\omega}}{16}\right)(X_{i})+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
S2.32
.

By Lemma
S2.15
and
S2.33
, to compute the asymptotic expansion of
M_{n}(\zeta)
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}
, we only need to plug in the expansion of
\Delta_{i}
as shown in Lemma
S2.33
into

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\bar{h}\left(X_{i}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{i}\right)-\bar{h}\left(X_{i}\right)\right)-\Delta_{i}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\Delta_{i}

for each
i\in[n]
. The concrete algebra is deferred to Supplementary Section
S3.1
.
∎

S2.9.8
Proof of Theorem 3.10 – part II

We formalize item (4) in the following proposition.

Proposition S2.34

(Expansion of
F_{n}^{\dagger*}
, cf. Proposition
S2.18
)
.

Under Assumption 3.9, there exists a deterministic
integer
N
such that, on the logarithmic-envelope event of
Remark
S2.31
and whenever
Condition A
holds, for every

n\geq N
,

\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}^{F^{\dagger}},

where
\left|\varepsilon_{n}^{F^{\dagger}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}^{\ddagger}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}^{\ddagger}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
.

Lemma S2.35
