(Tail bound of the solution in
F_{n}^{\dagger*}(-H_{n})
, cf. Lemma
S2.20
)
.

Under Assumption 3.9, define
\mathcal{Z}_{n}
as in (
S.11
)
and
\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}
as any optimizer of

\displaystyle\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}.

There exists a deterministic integer
N
such that, on the
logarithmic-envelope event of Remark
S2.31
and whenever

Condition A
holds,
\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}\in\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
for
every
n\geq N
.

Proof of Lemma
S2.35
.

Recall the definition of
F_{n}
in (
S.22
). On the
logarithmic-envelope event of Remark
S2.31
, the first three
derivatives of
\mathbf{h}
are bounded by deterministic logarithmic envelopes.
Hence, for a deterministic polylogarithmic sequence
b_{n}
,

\left|F_{n}(\zeta)-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right|\leq\frac{Cb_{n}}{n}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}^{4}\qquad\forall\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}.

On the boundary
\left\|\zeta\right\|_{2}=r_{n}
, the proof of
Lemma
S2.20
gives, for all sufficiently large
n
,

-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}(\zeta)\leq-\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{32}r_{n}^{2}.

Moreover,
r_{n}^{2}=O\{\log(n)\}
, so

Cb_{n}r_{n}^{4}/n=o(r_{n}^{2})
. Therefore

-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F_{n}^{\dagger}(\zeta)<0
throughout the boundary for
all sufficiently large
n
. Since the objective equals zero at

\zeta=\mathbf{0}
, every optimizer
\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}
is in

\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
S2.34
.

Lemma
S2.35
shows that the optimal solution
\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}
of

\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}

is achieved in
\inte(\mathcal{Z}_{n})
. Thus,
\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}
satisfies
-H_{n}-\mathcal{D}F_{n}^{\dagger}(\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})=\mathbf{0}
. Using this relation, we obtain

-\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}^{F^{\dagger}},

where
\left|\varepsilon_{n}^{F^{\dagger}}\right|\leq\delta_{n}^{\ddagger}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}^{\ddagger}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
. The concrete algebra is deferred to Supplementary Section
S3.2
.
∎

S2.9.9
Proof of Theorem 3.10 – combining steps

Proof of Theorem 3.10.

Applying Proposition
S2.5
, under Assumptions 2.4 and 2.5, we have

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}}\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-M_{n}(\zeta)\}.

(S.61)

Applying Proposition
S2.13
, under Assumptions 2.4
and 2.5 (the latter is included in Assumption 3.9), there is a deterministic
integer
N_{1}
such that, when
n\geq N_{1}
and
Condition A

holds,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:step5supMn_II}=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-M_{n}(\zeta)\}.

(S.62)

On the logarithmic-envelope event of Remark
S2.31
, applying
Proposition
S2.32
under Assumption 3.9 and

Condition A
gives a deterministic integer
N_{2}
such that, when

n\geq N_{2}
,

(
S.62
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)-\varepsilon^{M^{\dagger}}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}=\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\left\{-\zeta^{\top}H_{n}-F^{\dagger}_{n}(\zeta)\right\}+\varepsilon^{F^{\ddagger}}_{n},

(S.63)

where
\left|\varepsilon^{F^{\ddagger}}_{n}\right|\leq\delta_{n}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
.

On the same logarithmic-envelope event, Proposition
S2.34
,
under
Condition A
, gives a deterministic
integer
N_{3}
such that, when
n\geq N_{3}
,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:step5supMn3II}=\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{n},\xi_{n}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}^{F^{\dagger}}+\varepsilon^{F^{\ddagger}}_{n},

(S.64)

where
\left|\varepsilon^{F^{\dagger}}_{n}\right|\leq\delta^{\prime}_{n}
for a deterministic sequence
\delta^{\prime}_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
. Therefore, we obtain (12).

Finally, by the reasoning in item (1) of Section
S2.9.6
, there is
a deterministic integer
N_{4}
such that, when
n\geq N_{4}
, the intersection
of the event in
Condition A
and
the logarithmic-envelope event has probability at least
1-O(n^{-3/2})
,
which concludes the proof.
∎

S2.9.10
Proof of Theorem 3.11

Bartlett coefficient definitions.

For
k=1,2,3
, define

\displaystyle C_{k}=-2u_{k}^{-1}\sum_{r=k}^{3}B_{r},\qquad u_{k}=2^{k}\Gamma(k+\tfrac{1}{2})/\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}),

where

\displaystyle B_{0}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{k_{11}^{2}+k_{22}}{2}+\frac{4k_{11}k_{31}+k_{42}}{8}-\frac{5k_{31}^{2}}{24},

\displaystyle B_{1}={}

\displaystyle\frac{k_{11}^{2}+k_{22}}{2}-\frac{4k_{11}k_{31}+k_{42}}{4}+\frac{5k_{31}^{2}}{8},

\displaystyle B_{2}={}

\displaystyle\frac{4k_{11}k_{31}+k_{42}}{8}-\frac{5k_{31}^{2}}{8},

\displaystyle B_{3}={}

\displaystyle\frac{5k_{31}^{2}}{24}.

The cumulant coefficients are

\displaystyle k_{11}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{3}}{2\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}{2\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}},

\displaystyle k_{31}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{2}^{3/2}}+\frac{3\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\sqrt{\alpha_{2}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}},

\displaystyle k_{22}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{3\alpha_{3}\tilde{\alpha}_{3}}{2\alpha_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}+\frac{7\alpha_{3}^{2}}{4\alpha_{2}^{3}}+\frac{-6\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\mathds{E}[(h\mathcal{D}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\top})(X)]+3\tilde{\alpha}_{4}\alpha_{2}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3\mathds{E}[(h\mathcal{D}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\top})(X)]}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\alpha_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{3}^{2}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{4}},

\displaystyle k_{42}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\alpha_{4}}{\alpha_{2}^{2}}+\frac{12\alpha_{3}^{2}}{\alpha_{2}^{3}}-\frac{18\alpha_{3}\tilde{\alpha}_{3}}{\alpha_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}+\frac{12\mathds{E}[(h\mathcal{D}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\top})(X)]}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{12\alpha_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{4}-24\tilde{\alpha}_{3}\mathds{E}[(h\mathcal{D}h\Sigma\mathcal{D}h^{\top})(X)]}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{3}}+\frac{9\alpha_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{3}^{2}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{4}}.

Here

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}={}

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[h(X)^{j}],\quad j=1,2,3,4,

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{2}={}

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[(h_{\alpha}h_{\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}})(X)],

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{3}={}

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[(h_{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}h_{\alpha}h_{\beta}\Sigma_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\beta})(X)],

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{4}={}

\displaystyle-\mathds{E}[(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h_{\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}})(X)]-\frac{1}{3}\mathds{E}[(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\beta^{\prime}}h_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}})(X)]

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4\tilde{\alpha}_{2}}\left\{\mathds{E}[(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\beta^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}})(X)]\right\}^{2}.

Here
k_{\beta\gamma}
denotes the
\gamma
th-order term in the expansion of
the
\beta
th cumulant of the signed-root statistic associated with
(12). Under no location shift,
k_{11}
and
k_{31}

coincide with
k_{1}
and
k_{3}
in (
S.49
), while
k_{2}=0
.

Notation for the proof.
In addition to the quantities above, set

•

A_{1}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i}),~A_{2}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(X_{i})^{2}-\alpha_{2}
.

•

\tilde{A}_{2}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})^{\top}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2},~\tilde{A}_{3}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}^{2}h(X_{i})\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X_{i})^{\top}-\tilde{\alpha}_{3}
.

Proof of Theorem 3.11.

Using Theorem 3.10 and noting that when
d_{h}=1
,

\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{A_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{\tilde{A}_{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{2}}\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho},

for the
(1-\varrho)
-quantile
\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
of the chi-square distribution with one degree of freedom, we get

\displaystyle nR_{n}(h)-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{A_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{\tilde{A}_{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{2}}\left(\hat{\gamma}^{2}-\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\hat{\varepsilon}_{n},

(S.65)

where we recall that
\left|\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}\right|\leq\hat{\delta}_{n}
with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
, for a deterministic sequence
\hat{\delta}_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
,
\hat{\gamma}=\sqrt{n}\left(R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3}\right)
, and
R_{j}=O_{p}(n^{-j/2}),j=1,2,3
. Concretely,

\displaystyle R_{1}=\frac{A_{1}}{\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}},~R_{2}=-\frac{A_{1}A_{2}}{2\alpha_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}A_{1}^{2}}{2\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}},

\displaystyle R_{3}=\frac{3A_{1}A_{2}^{2}}{8\alpha_{2}^{\frac{5}{2}}}-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}A_{1}^{2}A_{2}}{4\alpha_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}A_{1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{2}}{\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{3}}+\frac{A_{1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{3}}{2\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{2}}+\left(-\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{3}^{2}}{8\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{4}}+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{4}}{2\alpha_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{3}}\right)A_{1}^{3},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{4}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\mathds{E}\left[\left(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h_{\beta^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\omega^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\right)(X)\right]-\frac{1}{3}\mathds{E}\left[\left(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\beta^{\prime}}h_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime\prime}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\right)(X)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4\tilde{\alpha}_{2}}\mathds{E}\left[\left(h_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}h_{\beta}h_{\beta^{\prime}}\Sigma_{\beta\alpha}\Sigma_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\right)(X)\right]^{2}.

Applying the Edgeworth expansion to
\hat{\gamma}

[
4
, Theorem 2]
and using the same reasoning as in (
S.35
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)\leq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=\int_{-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}}^{\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}}\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v+\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\int_{-\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}}^{\sqrt{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}}p(v)\phi(v)\mathrm{d}v}_{(F)}+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right),

where
\phi
is the density function of a standard normal distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and the polynomial
p
is

\displaystyle p(v)=\frac{k_{11}^{2}+k_{22}}{2}(v^{2}-1)+\frac{4k_{11}k_{31}+k_{42}}{24}(v^{4}-6v^{2}+3)+\frac{k_{31}^{2}}{72}(v^{6}-15v^{4}+45v^{2}-15).

The derivation of the constants
k
’s (the cumulants expansion of
\hat{\gamma}
) is collected in Section
S4.3
.

Then we rewrite part
(F)
in Theorem 3.11 as

\displaystyle(F)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=0}^{3}B_{r}G_{2r+1}(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho})=\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{3}C_{i}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)^{i},

where
G_{r}(\cdot)
,
g_{r}(\cdot)
, and
\chi^{2}_{r;1-\varrho}
are the CDF, the density, and the
(1-\varrho)
-quantile of a chi-square distribution with
r
degrees of freedom. The proof is complete.
∎

S2.9.11
Proof of Proposition 3.12

Proof of Proposition 3.12.

Using Theorem 3.11, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)\leq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=G_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right).

Let
c=\frac{1}{n}q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)/\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)\leq(1-c)\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{1}\left(\left(1-c\right)\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left((1-c)\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)q\left((1-c)\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}-\frac{1}{n}q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)-\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\frac{1}{n}g_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)q\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{1}\left(\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\varrho+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right),

where the second equality uses
c=O\left(n^{-1}\right)
and the third uses the
Taylor expansion of
G_{1}(\cdot)
around
\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
.
∎

S2.9.12
Proof of Proposition 3.13

Proof of Proposition 3.13.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(nR_{n}(h)\leq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}{\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}}nR_{n}(h)\leq\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{n}}{\mathcal{W}_{n}}nR_{n}(h)\leq\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right).

Let
S=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{n}}{\mathcal{W}_{n}}nR_{n}(h)
. Comparing with (
S.65
), where
A_{2}+\alpha_{2}=\mathcal{W}_{n}
and
\tilde{A}_{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{2}=\mathcal{V}_{n}
, we get

S=\hat{\gamma}^{2}+\frac{\mathcal{V}_{n}}{\mathcal{W}_{n}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}.

Recall the bounds derived in (
S.39
). Under Assumption 3.9,
h(X)
and
\mathcal{D}h(X)
have exponential moments, so
h(X)^{2}
and
\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}
have finite moments of all orders. Using the same truncation and concentration argument as in Lemma
S2.9
, we obtain

\left|\mathcal{W}_{n}-W\right|,\left|\mathcal{V}_{n}-V\right|=\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{\log(n)/n}\right)

with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
, where
W=\mathds{E}\left[h(X)^{2}\right]
and
V=\mathds{E}\left[\mathcal{D}h(X)\Sigma\mathcal{D}h(X)^{\top}\right]
. Therefore,
\left|\frac{\mathcal{V}_{n}}{\mathcal{W}_{n}}\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}\right|\leq\delta_{n}
with probability at least
1-O\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
, for a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right)
.

Then, the same reasoning as in (
S.35
) shows that
S
and

\hat{\gamma}^{2}
share the same Edgeworth expansion up to order
n^{-1}
.
Finally, applying
[
18
, Theorem 1]
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{3}C_{k}S^{k-1}\right)S\leq\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)=1-\varrho+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right),

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{3}C_{k}S^{k-1}\right)nR_{n}(h)\leq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\right)=1-\varrho+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

S2.10
Proofs in Section 4

Throughout this subsection, the significance level is written
\varrho
, while

\alpha
denotes the transport multiplier. The notation of
Section 4 is in force: the dual objective
F_{\delta}
and value

\Phi_{n}(\delta)
of (17), the localized value

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)
of (25), the matrix

A(x)=\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(x)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}
, the curvature constant
K_{\mathrm{curv}}

of (21), the eigenvalues
\ell_{n},U_{n}

of (23), the box

\mathcal{K}_{\delta}=[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]\times\mathbb{B}_{2}

of (24). We work on the
observable event
\ell_{n}>0
. The following elementary consequences of

0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
are used repeatedly; each follows from

K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}\leq(3+2\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})^{-1}\leq 1/5
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(B1)}\quad&\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\geq K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left(1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}\right)\geq 2K_{\mathrm{curv}},\qquad\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\geq\frac{2}{5}\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{\delta}},\qquad\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}-\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\leq\sqrt{\frac{U_{n}}{\delta}}\ ;\\
\text{(B2)}\quad&2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\geq\frac{3}{5}\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{\delta}}\ \text{ and }\ 2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\geq K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+2\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}),\qquad\forall\,\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\ ;\\
\text{(B3)}\quad&\frac{K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{U_{n}}}{2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\ell_{n}}}{2},\qquad\frac{2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}}{2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}}\leq\frac{5}{3},\qquad\forall\,\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\ ;\\
\text{(B4)}\quad&2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{7}{5}\sqrt{\frac{U_{n}}{\delta}},\qquad\forall\,\alpha\leq\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}\ .\end{split}

(S.66)

Indeed, (B1) restates
\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta_{0}}=K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})
and
K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}
; (B2) follows from

2\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}=\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}-2K_{\mathrm{curv}}
and (B1); the first part of (B3) uses

2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\geq K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+2\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})\geq 2K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}
, the second uses

\alpha\geq 2K_{\mathrm{curv}}
; and (B4) uses
2\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}=\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}+2K_{\mathrm{curv}}
and

K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}
. In particular,

\sqrt{\ell_{n}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta}\geq\frac{4}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}}>0
for
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
, and

\underline{\alpha}_{\delta_{0}}=K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})\geq 2K_{\mathrm{curv}}
: the condition

\delta\leq\delta_{0}
places the entire box strictly inside the regime
2\alpha>K_{\mathrm{curv}}
in which
the inner problems are strongly convex, with quantitative slack. When
K_{\mathrm{curv}}=0
these facts
hold trivially with
\delta_{0}=+\infty
.

S2.10.1
Proof of Proposition 4.1

Proof.

The growth condition needed for the DRO reformulation is already contained in
Assumption 2.5. Indeed, fix any
x_{0}\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
. Applying that assumption with
base point
x_{0}
and integrating the Jacobian along the segment from
x_{0}
to
x
gives

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{h}(x)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{h}(x_{0})\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(x_{0})\right\|_{2}\left\|x-x_{0}\right\|_{2}+\frac{\kappa_{1}(x_{0})}{2}\left\|x-x_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{x_{0}}\left(1+\left\|x-x_{0}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\right)

(S.67)

for some finite
C_{x_{0}}
. Thus no separate computational growth assumption is required,
but the moment image need not be closed at this quadratic growth rate. We therefore use its
closure below.

Step 1: the finite-dimensional moment image.

For
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})
, couple
\mathbb{P}
with
\mathbb{Q}_{n}
and apply the pointwise
inequality
\left\|x-x_{0}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\leq 2\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}+2\left\|X_{i}-x_{0}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}
to obtain

\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\!\left[\left\|X-x_{0}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\right]\leq 2\delta+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|X_{i}-x_{0}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}.

Together with (
S.67
), this ensures that every moment below is finite.
Moreover,
\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})
is convex because couplings can be mixed. Define its moment image by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\delta}\triangleq\left\{\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]:\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})\right\}\subset\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}.

(S.68)

This set is nonempty and convex, although it need not be closed. By definition,

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})}\left\|\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]\right\|_{2}=\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\left\|m\right\|_{2}.

(S.69)

Step 2: finite-dimensional distance duality.

Let
d_{\delta}\triangleq\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\left\|m\right\|_{2}
and let

m^{\star}
be the Euclidean projection of the origin onto the nonempty closed
convex set
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta}
. For every
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}
, a
sequence in
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
converging to
m^{\star}
gives

\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\xi^{\top}m\leq\xi^{\top}m^{\star}\leq d_{\delta}
.
If
d_{\delta}>0
, the projection inequality

(m-m^{\star})^{\top}m^{\star}\geq 0
for

m\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\delta}
shows that

\xi^{\star}=m^{\star}/d_{\delta}
attains the reverse bound; if
d_{\delta}=0
,

\xi=\mathbf{0}
does so. Consequently,

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\left\|m\right\|_{2}=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}\xi^{\top}m=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}\,\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})}\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\!\left[\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(X)\right].

(S.70)

This is the only minimax step required; it remains valid even when

\mathcal{M}_{\delta}
is not closed. Also,

\max_{\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(X)]=\left\|\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]\right\|_{2}
for each
\mathbb{P}
.

Step 3: Wasserstein DRO duality.

Fix
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}
and set
f_{\xi}(x)\triangleq\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)
. The function

-f_{\xi}
is continuous and
\mathbb{Q}_{n}
-integrable, and
(
S.67
) gives it a finite (possibly nonzero) quadratic
growth rate in the sense of
[
27
]
. Applying the
Wasserstein DRO strong duality
[
27
, Theorem 1]
to
-f_{\xi}
over

\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})
with radius
\delta>0
, and changing signs, gives

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})}\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[f_{\xi}(X)]=\sup_{\alpha\geq 0}\left\{-\alpha\delta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\inf_{x\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}}\left[f_{\xi}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\right]\right\}=\sup_{\alpha\geq 0}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi).

(S.71)

For multipliers below the effective quadratic growth rate, an inner infimum
on the right may equal
-\infty
; the extended-valued formulation above
includes such multipliers harmlessly. Taking the supremum over

\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}
and combining (
S.71
) with
(
S.70
), (
S.69
), and the
dual representation of the Euclidean norm proves all equalities in
(19) and their nonnegativity.

Step 4: equivalence of the threshold comparison.

By part (i) and
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq 0
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\inf_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})}\left\|\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]\right\|_{2}=0.

(S.72)

(
\Rightarrow
) Suppose
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta
. Choose
\mathbb{P}_{k}
with

\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=\mathbf{0}
and write

\rho_{k}\triangleq\mathds{W}_{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{P}_{k})\leq\delta+1/k
. If
\rho_{k}\leq\delta
,
put
t_{k}=1
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}=\mathbb{P}_{k}
. Otherwise put

t_{k}\triangleq\delta/\rho_{k}
and

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}\triangleq t_{k}\mathbb{P}_{k}+(1-t_{k})\mathbb{Q}_{n}
. Mixing a coupling for

(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{P}_{k})
with the diagonal coupling shows that

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})
, while

\mathds{E}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=(1-t_{k})\overline{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathbf{0},\qquad\overline{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i}),

because
t_{k}\to 1
whenever the second case occurs. Hence the infimum in
(
S.72
) is zero.

(
\Leftarrow
) Assumption 2.4 and finite dimensionality
provide fixed points
y_{1},\ldots,y_{J}\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
and
r>0
such that

\displaystyle r\mathbb{B}_{2}\subseteq\conv\{\mathbf{h}(y_{1}),\ldots,\mathbf{h}(y_{J})\}.

(S.73)

Choose
\mathbb{P}_{k}\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbb{Q}_{n})
with

m_{k}\triangleq\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]\to\mathbf{0}
. If some
m_{k}=\mathbf{0}
, then that

\mathbb{P}_{k}
is feasible for (4) and
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta
. Otherwise,
let
u_{k}\triangleq m_{k}/\left\|m_{k}\right\|_{2}
and choose a probability measure
S_{k}

supported on
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{J}\}
with

\mathds{E}_{S_{k}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=-ru_{k}
. Set

s_{k}\triangleq\frac{\left\|m_{k}\right\|_{2}}{r+\left\|m_{k}\right\|_{2}},\qquad\mathbb{P}_{k}^{0}\triangleq(1-s_{k})\mathbb{P}_{k}+s_{k}S_{k}.

Then
\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{k}^{0}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=\mathbf{0}
. Moreover,

C_{0}\triangleq\max_{i\in[n],j\in[J]}\left\|X_{i}-y_{j}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}<\infty
and the
product coupling give
\mathds{W}_{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},S_{k})\leq C_{0}
, so convexity of the
transport cost yields

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\mathds{W}_{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{P}_{k}^{0})\leq(1-s_{k})\delta+s_{k}C_{0}\leq\delta+s_{k}C_{0}\longrightarrow\delta.

Thus
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta
. Taking
complements in (
S.72
) proves
(20).
∎

S2.10.2
Proof of Proposition 4.2

Proof.

Fix
0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
. We first record two facts used throughout.

Fact 1 (Lipschitz Jacobian).
For all
x,y\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|A(x)-A(y)\right\|_{2}\leq K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|x-y\right\|_{\Sigma}.

(S.74)

Indeed, for unit vectors
u\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
,
v\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
, writing

x-y=\Sigma^{1/2}w
with
\left\|w\right\|_{2}=\left\|x-y\right\|_{\Sigma}
, the fundamental
theorem of calculus gives

\displaystyle\left|u^{\top}\left(A(x)-A(y)\right)v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|w^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\mathcal{D}^{2}\!\left(u^{\top}\mathbf{h}\right)(y+t(x-y))\Sigma^{1/2}v\right|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|w\right\|_{2},

by (22).

Fact 2 (spectral bounds).
For every
u\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(X_{i})^{\top}u\right\|_{2}^{2}=u^{\top}\mathcal{V}_{n}u
, so by
(23),

\displaystyle\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\left\|u\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(X_{i})^{\top}u\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{U_{n}}\left\|u\right\|_{2}.

(S.75)

Step 1: strong convexity of the inner problems (part (ii)).

Fix
\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}
, and
i\in[n]
. The
inner objective is twice continuously differentiable with Hessian

H_{i}(x)=\mathcal{D}^{2}(\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h})(x)+2\alpha\Sigma^{-1}
. For any

v=\Sigma^{1/2}w\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}
, (22) and

\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\leq 1
give

\left|v^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}(\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h})(x)v\right|\leq K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|w\right\|_{2}^{2}=K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|v\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}
, whence

\displaystyle\lambda(\alpha)\,\Sigma^{-1}\preccurlyeq H_{i}(x)\preccurlyeq(2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}})\,\Sigma^{-1}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}.

(S.76)

By (B2) of (
S.66
),

\lambda(\alpha)\geq 2\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}=\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}-2K_{\mathrm{curv}}\geq\frac{3}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}>0
, which is (28),
so the inner objective is
\lambda(\alpha)
-strongly convex and

(2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}})
-smooth with respect to
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\Sigma}
; strong
convexity implies coercivity and hence a unique minimizer
g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
.
The condition-ratio bound on
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
is (B3). For the displacement bound, the
first-order condition for

g_{i}\triangleq g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
gives

\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(g_{i}-X_{i})=-\frac{1}{2\alpha}A(g_{i})^{\top}\xi
, hence, by
(
S.74
) and
\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\leq 1
,

2\alpha\left\|g_{i}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}=\left\|A(g_{i})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|g_{i}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma},

so rearranging and applying (B2) yields

\displaystyle\left\|g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}\leq\frac{\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}}{\lambda(\alpha)}\leq\frac{5}{3}\,\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}}.

(S.77)

This proves part (ii).

Step 2: existence and localization of maximizers.

Evaluating each inner infimum in (17) at
x=X_{i}
gives,
with
\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(X_{i})
,

\displaystyle F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\leq-\alpha\delta+\xi^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\leq-\alpha\delta+\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\left\|\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\right\|_{2},

(S.78)

while
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\mathbf{0})=-\alpha\delta\leq 0
and
F_{\delta}(0,\mathbf{0})=0
,
so
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq 0
and the supremum in (17) is
unchanged if we restrict to the compact set

[0,\left\|\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{n}\right\|_{2}/\delta]\times\mathbb{B}_{2}
, on which the upper
semicontinuous function
F_{\delta}
(a pointwise infimum of affine functions
of
(\alpha,\xi)
, minus
\alpha\delta
) attains its maximum. This proves
attainment.

Now suppose
\Phi_{n}(\delta)>0
and let
(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})
be any
maximizer. Since
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\mathbf{0})\leq 0
, we have

\xi^{\star}\neq\mathbf{0}
. By the homogeneity (18), if

\left\|\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}<1
then
t\triangleq 1/\left\|\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}>1
gives the feasible
point
(t\alpha^{\star},t\xi^{\star})
with strictly larger value

t\Phi_{n}(\delta)>\Phi_{n}(\delta)
, a contradiction; hence

\left\|\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}=1
. Next, Assumption 2.4 provides

r_{\mathbf{h}}>0
with
\{u:\left\|u\right\|_{2}\leq r_{\mathbf{h}}\}\subseteq\conv\{\mathbf{h}(x):x\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}\}
. Since the infimum of a linear functional
over a set coincides with its infimum over the convex hull,

\displaystyle F_{\delta}(0,\xi^{\star})=\inf_{x\in\mathds{R}^{d_{X}}}(\xi^{\star})^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)\leq(\xi^{\star})^{\top}\left(-r_{\mathbf{h}}\xi^{\star}\right)=-r_{\mathbf{h}}<0<\Phi_{n}(\delta),

(S.79)

so
\alpha^{\star}>0
.

We next bracket
\alpha^{\star}
. More generally, fix
\xi\neq\mathbf{0}
and
suppose
\alpha^{\star}>0
maximizes

G_{\xi}(\alpha)\triangleq F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)
over
[0,\infty)
. Write

\alpha_{c}\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}.

By (B1),

2\alpha_{c}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}=\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}(\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}-K_{\mathrm{curv}})>0
.
Thus, for every
\alpha\geq\alpha_{c}
, each inner objective is strongly
convex and has a unique minimizer
g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
. Define

D_{\xi}(\alpha)\triangleq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The classical envelope formula and the inner first-order conditions give

\displaystyle G_{\xi}^{\prime}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=-\delta+D_{\xi}(\alpha)^{2},

(S.80)

\displaystyle 2\alpha D_{\xi}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(g_{i}(\alpha,\xi))^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(X_{i})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\left(A(g_{i}(\alpha,\xi))-A(X_{i})\right)^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}D_{\xi}(\alpha).

(S.81)

Here the last two inequalities use Minkowski,
(
S.75
), and
(
S.74
). Rearranging gives

D_{\xi}(\alpha)\geq\frac{\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}}{2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}}.

Consequently, whenever

\alpha\in[\alpha_{c},\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{\delta})
,
the definition of
\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}
gives

2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}<\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}
, and hence

D_{\xi}(\alpha)>\sqrt{\delta}
and
G_{\xi}^{\prime}(\alpha)>0
.

Because
G_{\xi}
is concave on
[0,\infty)
and differentiable at

\alpha_{c}
, its supporting-line inequality gives, for
0\leq\beta<\alpha_{c}
,

G_{\xi}(\beta)\leq G_{\xi}(\alpha_{c})+G_{\xi}^{\prime}(\alpha_{c})(\beta-\alpha_{c})<G_{\xi}(\alpha_{c}).

Together with strict increase on

[\alpha_{c},\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{\delta})
, this excludes every
maximizer below
\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}
and proves

\displaystyle\alpha^{\star}\geq\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\,\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}.

(S.82)

In particular,
2\alpha^{\star}>K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}
, so
G_{\xi}
is differentiable
at the positive unconstrained maximizer
\alpha^{\star}
and

\displaystyle D_{\xi}(\alpha^{\star})^{2}=\delta.

(S.83)

Writing
g_{i}^{\star}\triangleq g_{i}(\alpha^{\star},\xi)
, the inner first-order
condition gives

\displaystyle 2\alpha^{\star}\left\|g_{i}^{\star}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}=\left\|A(g_{i}^{\star})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}.

(S.84)

Minkowski, (
S.75
),
(
S.74
), and
(
S.83
) now yield the two-sided comparison

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{\ell_{n}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta}\right)\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\leq 2\alpha^{\star}\sqrt{\delta}\leq\left(\sqrt{U_{n}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta}\right)\left\|\xi\right\|_{2},

(S.85)

so
\alpha^{\star}\leq\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\,\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}
. Applying the
two-sided bracket to the joint
maximizer
(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})
, which has
\left\|\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}=1
and

\alpha^{\star}>0
maximizing
G_{\xi^{\star}}
, yields

(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})\in\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
.

Step 3: the localization identity (part (i)).

The maximum in (25) is attained because
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
is
compact and
F_{\delta}
upper semicontinuous, and

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)\leq\Phi_{n}(\delta)
trivially. If

\Phi_{n}(\delta)>0
, Step 2 shows that some (indeed every) maximizer of

\Phi_{n}(\delta)
lies in
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
, whence

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)=\Phi_{n}(\delta)>0

and (26) holds. If
\Phi_{n}(\delta)=0
, then

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)\leq 0
, so

\max\{\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta),0\}=0=\Phi_{n}(\delta)
. Combining
(26) with
Proposition 4.1(ii) yields
(27).

For the sharper boundary statement used after the proposition, suppose

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})<\delta
. By the definition of
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})
, there is a
distribution
\mathbb{P}_{0}
satisfying
\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]=\mathbf{0}
and a coupling
with
\mathbb{Q}_{n}
whose transportation cost
r
is strictly smaller than
\delta
.
Weak duality then gives, for every
(\alpha,\xi)\in\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
,

F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\leq-\alpha\delta+\xi^{\top}\mathds{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}[\mathbf{h}(X)]+\alpha r=-\alpha(\delta-r)\leq-\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}(\delta-r)<0.

Hence
\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta)<0
. Together with
(27), this proves

\{\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta)=0\}\subseteq\{R_{n}(\mathbf{h})=\delta\}
.

Step 4: strong concavity in
\xi
(part (iii)).

Fix
\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}
and write
g_{i}=g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
for
the unique minimizers from Step 1, and

D_{g}\triangleq\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The first-order condition
2\alpha\left\|g_{i}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}=\left\|A(g_{i})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}
and the triangle inequality in the empirical
L^{2}

norm give, using (
S.75
),
(
S.74
), and
\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle 2\alpha D_{g}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(g_{i})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{U_{n}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}D_{g},\qquad\text{whence}\qquad D_{g}\leq\frac{\sqrt{U_{n}}}{\lambda(\alpha)}.

(S.86)

Transferring the empirical nondegeneracy from the atoms
X_{i}
to the
minimizers
g_{i}
, for every
u\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(g_{i})^{\top}u\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\left\|u\right\|_{2}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|u\right\|_{2}D_{g}\geq\left(\sqrt{\ell_{n}}-\frac{K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{U_{n}}}{\lambda(\alpha)}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{2},

(S.87)

and the coefficient is positive: by (B2),

\lambda(\alpha)\geq K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+2\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})\geq K_{\mathrm{curv}}(1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})
, so (when
K_{\mathrm{curv}}>0
)

\displaystyle\frac{K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{U_{n}}}{\lambda(\alpha)}\leq\frac{\sqrt{U_{n}}}{1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}}=\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\,\frac{\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}}{1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}}\leq\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\left(1-\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}}}\right),

(S.88)

whence

\sqrt{\ell_{n}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{U_{n}}/\lambda(\alpha)\geq\sqrt{\ell_{n}}/(1+\sqrt{\varkappa_{n}})>0
; for
K_{\mathrm{curv}}=0
the coefficient
equals
\sqrt{\ell_{n}}
and the same lower bound holds trivially. This also
proves the second inequality in (29) after
squaring and dividing by
2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}
.

It remains to bound the Hessian of
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\cdot)
. Since

2\alpha>K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}
holds on an open neighborhood of
\mathbb{B}_{2}
, the
minimizers
g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
are unique there, and by

\mathbf{h}\in C^{3}
(Assumption 2.5),
H_{i}(g_{i})\succ 0
, and the implicit
function theorem,
\xi\mapsto g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
is continuously
differentiable with

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}g_{i}=-H_{i}(g_{i})^{-1}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(g_{i})^{\top}
. Danskin’s envelope theorem
for the (unique-minimizer) infimum gives

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(g_{i}(\alpha,\xi))^{\top}
,
and differentiating once more,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{2}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(g_{i})H_{i}(g_{i})^{-1}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(g_{i})^{\top}.

(S.89)

The upper bound in (
S.76
) implies

H_{i}(g_{i})^{-1}\succcurlyeq(2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}})^{-1}\Sigma
, so for every

u\in\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
,

u^{\top}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(g_{i})H_{i}(g_{i})^{-1}\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(g_{i})^{\top}u\geq\frac{\left\|A(g_{i})^{\top}u\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}}.

Averaging over
i
and applying (
S.87
) yields

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{2}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\preccurlyeq-\mu(\alpha)I_{d_{h}}\qquad\text{for every }\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}.

Since
\mathbb{B}_{2}
is convex, integrating this bound along line segments in

\mathbb{B}_{2}
shows that
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\cdot)
is

\mu(\alpha)
-strongly concave on
\mathbb{B}_{2}
, proving part (iii).
∎

S2.10.3
Localization and margin lemmas

Lemma S2.36

(Upper bound for the optimal transport multiplier)
.

Suppose Assumption 2.5 holds and
(21) is satisfied. Fix
\delta>0
and

\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}
. Every maximizer of

\alpha\mapsto F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)
over
[0,\infty)
satisfies

\displaystyle\alpha^{\star}\leq\frac{\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{U_{n}}{\delta}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\right)\leq\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}.

(S.90)

Proof.

If
\alpha^{\star}=0
the claim is trivial, so assume
\alpha^{\star}>0
. The
claim is immediate if

2\alpha^{\star}\leq K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}
. Otherwise each inner objective is
strongly convex in a neighborhood of
\alpha^{\star}
, so its unique minimizer

g_{i}^{\star}
obeys the classical envelope formula. Since
\alpha^{\star}
is
a positive unconstrained maximizer,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{\star}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}=\delta.

The inner first-order conditions, Minkowski,
(
S.75
), and (
S.74
) give

2\alpha^{\star}\sqrt{\delta}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|A(g_{i}^{\star})^{\top}\xi\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\sqrt{U_{n}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta}\right)\left\|\xi\right\|_{2},

which is (
S.90
). Thus the bound holds on both sides of
the strong-convexity threshold, without requiring subquadratic coercivity.
∎

For the uniform curvature bounds, stopping rules, and complexity ledger below, define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu_{\delta}&\triangleq\frac{\ell_{n}}{8}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{U_{n}}},\qquad\mathsf{L}_{\delta}\triangleq\frac{15U_{n}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}},\qquad\frac{\mathsf{L}_{\delta}}{\mu_{\delta}}=30\varkappa_{n}^{3/2},\\
\Lambda_{\delta}&\triangleq 3\varkappa_{n}\delta,\qquad\mathsf{s}_{n}\triangleq\max_{i\in[n]}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2},\qquad\bar{\mathsf{m}}_{n}^{2}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2},\\
\mathsf{M}_{\delta}&\triangleq\bar{\mathsf{m}}_{n}+\frac{20}{9}\,d_{h}U_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}}.\end{split}

(S.91)

Thus
\mu_{\delta}
and
\mathsf{L}_{\delta}
are box-uniform strong-concavity and smoothness
moduli in
\xi
,
\Lambda_{\delta}
bounds
|\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}|
on
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
,

\mathsf{M}_{\delta}
bounds
\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}\right\|_{2}
, and the data scales
\mathsf{s}_{n}
and

\bar{\mathsf{m}}_{n}
enter the complexity bound only through logarithms. These claims are
established in the next lemma.

Lemma S2.37

(Joint curvature structure of the dual objective)
.

Suppose Assumption 2.5 holds,
(21) is satisfied, and
\ell_{n}>0
. Let

0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
. Write
z=(\alpha,\xi)
and let

\Omega\triangleq\{(\alpha,\xi):2\alpha>K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}\}
, an open convex cone
containing
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
.

(i)

(Concavity and homogeneity.)

F_{\delta}
is jointly
concave and upper semicontinuous on
[0,\infty)\times\mathds{R}^{d_{h}}
, and
positively homogeneous of degree one:

F_{\delta}(tz)=tF_{\delta}(z)
for all
t>0
.

(ii)

(Smoothness.)
On
\Omega
, each inner problem has a
unique minimizer
g_{i}(z)
which is
C^{1}
in
z
, and

F_{\delta}\in C^{2}(\Omega)
with

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}(z)=-\delta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}(z)-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2},\qquad\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}\left(g_{i}(z)\right).

(S.92)

(iii)

(Failure of joint strong concavity.)
For every

z\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{2}F_{\delta}(z)\,[z,z]=0.

(S.93)

Consequently
F_{\delta}
is not strongly concave on any convex subset of

\Omega
with nonempty interior. In particular, if

\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}<\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}
, then
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}

has nonempty interior and joint strong concavity fails on
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
.

(iv)

(Uniform partial curvature on the box.)
For every

(\alpha,\xi)\in\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
,

\displaystyle-\mathsf{L}_{\delta}I_{d_{h}}\preccurlyeq\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{2}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\preccurlyeq-\mu_{\delta}I_{d_{h}},\qquad\left|\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\right|\leq\Lambda_{\delta},\qquad\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\right\|_{2}\leq\mathsf{M}_{\delta}.

(S.94)

(v)

(Profile.)

G_{\delta}(\alpha)=\max_{\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)
is concave and

\Lambda_{\delta}
-Lipschitz on

[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]
, and for each such

\alpha
the maximizer
\xi^{\star}(\alpha)
is unique with the partial
quadratic growth

\displaystyle G_{\delta}(\alpha)-F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)\geq\frac{\mu_{\delta}}{2}\left\|\xi-\xi^{\star}(\alpha)\right\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\forall\,\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}.

(S.95)

Proof.

(i)
For fixed
x
and
i
, the map

(\alpha,\xi)\mapsto\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}
is affine;
an infimum of affine functions is concave and upper semicontinuous, and so
is the average, minus the linear term
\alpha\delta
. Homogeneity holds
because
\inf_{x}\{t\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+t\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\}=t\inf_{x}\{\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\}
for
t>0
, and

-(t\alpha)\delta=t(-\alpha\delta)
.

(ii)
Fix
z_{0}=(\alpha_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Omega
. On a neighborhood of

z_{0}
contained in
\Omega
, the inner Hessian satisfies

\mathcal{D}^{2}\left(\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}\right)(x)+2\alpha\Sigma^{-1}\succcurlyeq(2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{2})\Sigma^{-1}\succ 0

by (22) (applied to the unit vector

\xi/\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}
and scaled), so the inner objective is strongly convex,
coercive, and has a unique minimizer
g_{i}(z)
. The first-order condition

\Psi_{i}(x;z)\triangleq\mathcal{D}\mathbf{h}(x)^{\top}\xi+2\alpha\Sigma^{-1}(x-X_{i})=\mathbf{0}
has a
continuously differentiable defining map (
\mathbf{h}\in C^{3}
by
Assumption 2.5) whose
x
-Jacobian at
g_{i}(z)
is the inner Hessian,
which is nonsingular on
\Omega
; the implicit function theorem

[
40
, Theorem 3.2.1]
then makes
g_{i}
locally
C^{1}
.
Since the minimizer is unique, Danskin’s theorem yields that

\phi_{i}(z)\triangleq\min_{x}\{\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\}
is
differentiable with

\nabla\phi_{i}(z)=\left(\left\|g_{i}(z)-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2},\ \mathbf{h}(g_{i}(z))\right)
, which is

C^{1}
because
g_{i}
is; hence
\phi_{i}\in C^{2}(\Omega)
,

F_{\delta}\in C^{2}(\Omega)
, and (
S.92
) holds.

(iii)
Each
\phi_{i}
is positively homogeneous of degree one on the
cone
\Omega
by the computation in (i). Differentiating

\phi_{i}(tz)=t\phi_{i}(z)
in
z
gives
\nabla\phi_{i}(tz)=\nabla\phi_{i}(z)
for

t>0
; differentiating this identity in
t
at
t=1
gives

\mathcal{D}^{2}\phi_{i}(z)\,z=\mathbf{0}
. Since the term
-\alpha\delta
is linear, the same
holds for
F_{\delta}
, proving (
S.93
). If

F_{\delta}
were
\mu
-strongly concave on a convex set

\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Omega
with interior point
\bar{z}
, then

s\mapsto F_{\delta}(\bar{z}+s\bar{z})
, defined for
|s|
small, would satisfy

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}s^{2}}F_{\delta}(\bar{z}+s\bar{z})\big|_{s=0}=\mathcal{D}^{2}F_{\delta}(\bar{z})[\bar{z},\bar{z}]\leq-\mu\left\|\bar{z}\right\|_{2}^{2}<0
,
contradicting (
S.93
).

(iv)
The upper bound

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{2}F_{\delta}\preccurlyeq-\mu(\alpha)I
with

\mu(\alpha)
as in (29) is
Proposition 4.2(iii). On the box, (B3) gives

\sqrt{\ell_{n}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{U_{n}}/(2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}})\geq\sqrt{\ell_{n}}/2
and (B4) gives

2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{7}{5}\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}
, whence

\mu(\alpha)\geq\frac{\ell_{n}/4}{(7/5)\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}}=\frac{5\ell_{n}}{28}\sqrt{\delta/U_{n}}\geq\mu_{\delta}
. For the smoothness bound,
(
S.89
) and

H_{i}(g_{i})^{-1}\preccurlyeq(2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}})^{-1}\Sigma

(from (
S.76
)) give

\mathbf{0}\succcurlyeq\mathcal{D}^{2}_{\xi}F_{\delta}\succcurlyeq-\frac{1}{2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A(g_{i})A(g_{i})^{\top},

and, by the triangle inequality in the product
L^{2}
space,
(
S.74
), (
S.86
), and (B3),

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A(g_{i})A(g_{i})^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{U_{n}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\,\frac{\sqrt{U_{n}}}{2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}}\right)^{\!2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{U_{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{\ell_{n}}}{2}\right)^{\!2}\leq\frac{9}{4}\,U_{n},

so that, using (B2),

\left\|\mathcal{D}^{2}_{\xi}F_{\delta}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{9U_{n}/4}{(3/5)\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}}=\mathsf{L}_{\delta}
. For the
\alpha
-derivative,
(
S.92
) and (
S.86
) give

-\delta\leq\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}\leq-\delta+U_{n}/(2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}})^{2}\leq-\delta+\frac{25}{9}\varkappa_{n}\delta
, and since
\varkappa_{n}\geq 1
,

\left|\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}\right|\leq\frac{25}{9}\varkappa_{n}\delta\leq\Lambda_{\delta}
. Finally, by (
S.77
) and
(
S.74
), the segment from
X_{i}
to
g_{i}
satisfies

\sup_{y\in[X_{i},g_{i}]}\left\|A(y)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}(1+\tfrac{5}{3}K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}})\leq\tfrac{4}{3}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}
, whence

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{h}(g_{i})-\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{4}{3}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\left\|g_{i}-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}\leq\frac{20}{9}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}},

(S.96)

using
\left\|\mathbf{h}(y^{\prime})-\mathbf{h}(y)\right\|_{2}=\|\int_{0}^{1}A(y+t(y^{\prime}-y))\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(y^{\prime}-y)\mathrm{d}t\|_{2}\leq\sup_{[y,y^{\prime}]}\left\|A\right\|_{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-y\right\|_{\Sigma}
. Averaging over
i
, and using

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{F}^{2}=\Tr(\mathcal{V}_{n})\leq d_{h}U_{n}
together with

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\|\mathbf{h}(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq\bar{\mathsf{m}}_{n}
, gives

\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}\right\|_{2}\leq\mathsf{M}_{\delta}
.

(v)
Concavity of
G_{\delta}
follows from joint concavity of

F_{\delta}
by partial maximization over the convex set
\mathbb{B}_{2}
.
Uniqueness of
\xi^{\star}(\alpha)
and (
S.95
) follow from

\mu_{\delta}
-strong concavity in
\xi
on the convex set
\mathbb{B}_{2}

(part (iv)); the Lipschitz bound follows because, for

\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
in the box,

\left|G_{\delta}(\alpha)-G_{\delta}(\alpha^{\prime})\right|\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}\left|F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)-F_{\delta}(\alpha^{\prime},\xi)\right|\leq\Lambda_{\delta}\left|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}\right|
by part (iv).
∎

Remark S2.38

(Degeneracy along homogeneous rays)
.

Identity (
S.93
) is Euler’s relation for the

1
-homogeneous function
F_{\delta}+\alpha\delta
and shows that its Hessian is
singular along every homogeneous ray in the original
(\alpha,\xi)

parameterization. Consequently, whenever an optimizer can be contracted
along a nontrivial feasible homogeneous ray, a quadratic-growth (PL-type)
constant on
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
is necessarily controlled by the optimal value.
Quantitatively, suppose that
z^{\star}=(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})
maximizes

F_{\delta}
over
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
with value
\Phi_{n}(\delta)>0
and that

\alpha^{\star}>\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}
. Then, along the feasible segment

t\mapsto tz^{\star}
,
t\in[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}/\alpha^{\star},1]
, one has

F_{\delta}(z^{\star})-F_{\delta}(tz^{\star})=(1-t)\Phi_{n}(\delta)
while

\left\|z^{\star}-tz^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}=(1-t)^{2}\left\|z^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}
, so any quadratic
growth constant is at most

\Phi_{n}(\delta)\big/\left((1-\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}/\alpha^{\star})\left\|z^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
. Along sequences for which

1-\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}/\alpha^{\star}
is bounded away from zero, this
bound vanishes as the decision boundary
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\downarrow 0
is
approached and is
O\left(\Phi_{n}(\delta)\,\delta/\ell_{n}\right)
, since

\left\|z^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta}^{2}\gtrsim\ell_{n}/\delta
.
A one-dimensional witness of the same
phenomenon in the
\alpha
direction alone is obtained as follows: for
\mathbf{h}(x)=x
,

\Sigma=I_{1}
, one computes

G_{\delta}(\alpha)=-\alpha\delta+\left|\bar{X}_{n}\right|-1/(4\alpha)
on the relevant
range, whose curvature
\left|G_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)\right|=1/(2\alpha^{3})\asymp\delta^{3/2}
vanishes at the rate
n^{-3/2}
when

\delta\asymp 1/n
. This is why Theorem 4.3treats
\alpha
by a one-dimensional search on the concave,

\Lambda_{\delta}
-Lipschitz profile
G_{\delta}
rather than by joint
gradient ascent.

Remark S2.39

(Euler identity as an implementation check)
.

On
\Omega
the gradients (
S.92
) satisfy

\alpha\,\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)+\xi^{\top}\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)=F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)
, an exact
identity that provides a costless correctness test for implementations of
the inner solver.

Lemma S2.40

(Two-sided margin for the dual value)
.

Suppose Assumptions 2.4 and 2.5 hold,
(21) is
satisfied, and
\ell_{n}>0
.

(i)

The map
\delta\mapsto\Phi_{n}(\delta)
is convex, nonincreasing,
and nonnegative on
(0,\infty)
, with
\Phi_{n}(\delta)=0
if and only if

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta
.

(ii)

(Lower bound.)
For all

0<\delta<\delta^{\prime}\leq\min\{R_{n}(\mathbf{h}),\delta_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta^{\prime}}\left(\delta^{\prime}-\delta\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{\delta^{\prime}}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\right)\left(\delta^{\prime}-\delta\right).

(S.97)

(iii)

(Upper bound.)
For all
0<\delta<R_{n}(\mathbf{h})
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{n}(\delta)\leq\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}\left(R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{U_{n}}{\delta}}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\right)\left(R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\right).

(S.98)

Proof.

Write
F_{0}(\alpha,\xi)\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\inf_{x}\{\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\}
, so that

F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi)=F_{0}(\alpha,\xi)-\alpha\delta
and

\Phi_{n}(s)=\sup_{\alpha\geq 0,\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{2}}\{F_{0}(\alpha,\xi)-\alpha s\}

is a supremum of affine functions of
s
, hence convex; it is nonincreasing
because the affine pieces have nonpositive slopes
-\alpha
. Nonnegativity
and the sign characterization are
Proposition 4.1. This proves (i).

(ii) Fix
\delta^{\prime}\in(\delta,\ R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\wedge\delta_{0}]
and take any

\delta^{\prime\prime}\in(\delta,\delta^{\prime})
; then
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})>\delta^{\prime\prime}
, so

\Phi_{n}(\delta^{\prime\prime})>0
, and by
Proposition 4.2(i) (applicable since

\delta^{\prime\prime}<\delta_{0}
) there is a maximizer
(\alpha^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})
of

\Phi_{n}(\delta^{\prime\prime})
with
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta^{\prime\prime}}
. Using
this point as a feasible candidate at radius
\delta
,

\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq F_{0}(\alpha^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})-\alpha^{\prime\prime}\delta=\left[F_{0}(\alpha^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})-\alpha^{\prime\prime}\delta^{\prime\prime}\right]+\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}-\delta)=\Phi_{n}(\delta^{\prime\prime})+\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}-\delta)\geq\underline{\alpha}_{\delta^{\prime\prime}}\left(\delta^{\prime\prime}-\delta\right),

where the last step uses
\Phi_{n}(\delta^{\prime\prime})\geq 0
. Since

\delta^{\prime\prime}\mapsto\underline{\alpha}_{\delta^{\prime\prime}}(\delta^{\prime\prime}-\delta)
is continuous,
letting
\delta^{\prime\prime}\uparrow\delta^{\prime}
gives (
S.97
).

(iii) Since
0<\delta<R_{n}(\mathbf{h})
, we have
\Phi_{n}(\delta)>0
by
Proposition 4.1(ii); let

(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})
be a maximizer (existence by
Proposition 4.2(i) when
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
; for

\delta>\delta_{0}
existence still holds by the compactification argument in
Step 2 of its proof, and Lemma
S2.36
applies for every

\delta>0
). Then, with
\rho\triangleq R_{n}(\mathbf{h})
, using the candidate

(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})
at radius
\rho
,

0=\Phi_{n}(\rho)\geq F_{0}(\alpha^{\star},\xi^{\star})-\alpha^{\star}\rho=\Phi_{n}(\delta)-\alpha^{\star}(\rho-\delta),

where
\Phi_{n}(\rho)=0
by part (i). Hence

\Phi_{n}(\delta)\leq\alpha^{\star}(\rho-\delta)\leq\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}(\rho-\delta)
by
Lemma
S2.36
.
∎

Corollary S2.41

(Decision band of an
\varepsilon
-accurate dual solve)
.

In the setting of Lemma
S2.40
, let
0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}/2
and

0\leq\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{\ell_{n}\delta}
. If

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})>\delta
and
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\leq 2\varepsilon
, then

\displaystyle R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\leq 5\,\varepsilon\,\sqrt{\frac{2\delta}{\ell_{n}}}.

(S.99)

Conversely, by (
S.98
), whenever

0<R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\leq 2\varepsilon\big/\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}
one has
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\leq 2\varepsilon
; hence
the width (
S.99
) is sharp up to the factor

5\sqrt{2\varkappa_{n}}
.

Proof.

Suppose first
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\geq 2\delta
. Then

\delta^{\prime}\triangleq 2\delta\leq\min\{R_{n}(\mathbf{h}),\delta_{0}\}

in (
S.97
) gives, using

K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/(2\delta)}
(valid since
2\delta\leq\delta_{0}
),

\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{2\delta}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\right)\delta\geq\frac{2}{5}\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{2\delta}}\,\delta=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}\delta}>\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\ell_{n}\delta}\geq 2\varepsilon,

contradicting
\Phi_{n}(\delta)\leq 2\varepsilon
. Hence
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})<2\delta
, and

\delta^{\prime}\triangleq R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq 2\delta\leq\delta_{0}

in (
S.97
) gives

2\varepsilon\geq\Phi_{n}(\delta)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{R_{n}(\mathbf{h})}}-K_{\mathrm{curv}}\right)\left(R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\right)\geq\frac{2}{5}\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{2\delta}}\left(R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta\right),

using again
K_{\mathrm{curv}}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/R_{n}(\mathbf{h})}
(valid since

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})\leq\delta_{0}
). Rearranging yields (
S.99
).
∎

S2.10.4
Algorithmic building blocks and pseudocode

Lemma S2.42

(Inner solver and error propagation)
.

In the setting of Proposition 4.2, fix

(\alpha,\xi)\in\mathcal{K}_{\delta}
and
i\in[n]
, and write

\psi_{i}(x)\triangleq\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}
,

m\triangleq 2\alpha-K_{\mathrm{curv}}
,
M\triangleq 2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}
,
g_{i}\triangleq g_{i}(\alpha,\xi)
.
Consider the preconditioned gradient iteration

x^{(k+1)}=x^{(k)}-\frac{1}{M}\Sigma\nabla\psi_{i}(x^{(k)})
started at

x^{(0)}=X_{i}
. Then:

(i)

\psi_{i}(x^{(k)})-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\left(1-\frac{m}{M}\right)^{k}\left(\psi_{i}(X_{i})-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\right)\leq\left(\frac{2}{5}\right)^{k}\,\frac{2\,\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{U_{n}\delta}}{\ell_{n}}
.

(ii)

If
\tilde{g}_{i}
satisfies

\psi_{i}(\tilde{g}_{i})-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\varepsilon_{x}
with

0\leq\varepsilon_{x}\leq\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}\leq 2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{x}}\left(\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}\right)^{\!1/4},\qquad\left\|\mathbf{h}(\tilde{g}_{i})-\mathbf{h}(g_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq 4\,\mathsf{s}_{n}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{x}}\left(\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}\right)^{\!1/4}\triangleq b(\varepsilon_{x}),

(S.100)

and
0\leq\psi_{i}(\tilde{g}_{i})-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\varepsilon_{x}
transfers to the
dual objective: replacing every
g_{i}
by
\tilde{g}_{i}
produces

\tilde{F}_{\delta}
with
F_{\delta}\leq\tilde{F}_{\delta}\leq F_{\delta}+\varepsilon_{x}
and a
\xi
-gradient surrogate

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\mathbf{h}(\tilde{g}_{i})
within
b(\varepsilon_{x})
of

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}
in Euclidean norm.

Proof.

By Proposition 4.2(ii),
\psi_{i}
is
m
-strongly
convex and
M
-smooth with respect to
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\Sigma}
, with

M/m\leq 5/3
.
(i)
The descent step gives, by
M
-smoothness in
the
\Sigma
-geometry,

\psi_{i}(x^{(k+1)})\leq\psi_{i}(x^{(k)})-\frac{1}{2M}\nabla\psi_{i}(x^{(k)})^{\top}\Sigma\nabla\psi_{i}(x^{(k)})
, while

m
-strong convexity gives the Polyak–Łojasiewicz bound

\psi_{i}(x)-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\frac{1}{2m}\nabla\psi_{i}(x)^{\top}\Sigma\nabla\psi_{i}(x)
; combining, the optimality gap contracts by the factor

1-m/M\leq 2/5
per step. The initial gap is bounded by smoothness and
(
S.77
):

\psi_{i}(X_{i})-\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\frac{M}{2}\left\|X_{i}-g_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\leq\frac{7}{10}\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}\cdot\frac{25}{9}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}\frac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}\leq\frac{2\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{U_{n}\delta}}{\ell_{n}}
, using (B2) and (B4).

(ii)
Strong convexity gives

\frac{m}{2}\left\|\tilde{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{x}
, so

\left\|\tilde{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}\leq\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{x}/m}\leq\sqrt{\frac{10}{3}\varepsilon_{x}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}}\leq 2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{x}}(\delta/\ell_{n})^{1/4}
by (B2). Under the standing bound

\varepsilon_{x}\leq\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}
, this is at most

2\mathsf{s}_{n}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}
, so every point
y
on the segment from

g_{i}
to
\tilde{g}_{i}
satisfies, by
(
S.77
) and (
S.74
),

\left\|A(y)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}+K_{\mathrm{curv}}\left(\tfrac{5}{3}\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}}+2\mathsf{s}_{n}\sqrt{\tfrac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}}\right)\leq\left\|A(X_{i})\right\|_{2}+\tfrac{11}{3}\,\mathsf{s}_{n}K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\tfrac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}}\leq 2\,\mathsf{s}_{n},

using
K_{\mathrm{curv}}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}}\leq 1/5
. The fundamental theorem of calculus
along that segment then gives

\left\|\mathbf{h}(\tilde{g}_{i})-\mathbf{h}(g_{i})\right\|_{2}\leq 2\mathsf{s}_{n}\left\|\tilde{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}\leq b(\varepsilon_{x})
. The value statement is
immediate from
\psi_{i}(g_{i})\leq\psi_{i}(\tilde{g}_{i})\leq\psi_{i}(g_{i})+\varepsilon_{x}

termwise, and the gradient statement follows by averaging
(
S.100
) over
i
and comparing
with (
S.92
).
∎

Lemma S2.43

(Inexact projected gradient ascent in
\xi
)
.

In the setting of Proposition 4.2, fix

\alpha\in[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]
and a target

\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,\mathsf{M}_{\delta}+\mathsf{L}_{\delta}]
, and set

r\triangleq\frac{\varepsilon_{G}}{\mathsf{M}_{\delta}+\mathsf{L}_{\delta}},\qquad T\triangleq\Big\lceil 30\,\varkappa_{n}^{3/2}\ln\frac{2}{r}\Big\rceil,\qquad\varepsilon_{x}\triangleq\min\left\{\varepsilon_{G},\ \mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{\delta}{\ell_{n}}},\ \left(\frac{\mu_{\delta}\,r}{16\,\mathsf{s}_{n}}\right)^{\!2}\sqrt{\tfrac{\ell_{n}}{\delta}}\right\}.

Run, from
\xi^{(0)}=\mathbf{0}
, the iteration

\xi^{(t+1)}=\Pi_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\!\left(\xi^{(t)}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{L}_{\delta}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}\left(\tilde{g}_{i}^{(t)}\right)\right),\qquad t=0,\ldots,T-1,

where each
\tilde{g}_{i}^{(t)}
is an
\varepsilon_{x}
-accurate inner solution at

(\alpha,\xi^{(t)})
in the sense of Lemma
S2.42
(ii). Then

G_{\delta}(\alpha)-F_{\delta}\left(\alpha,\xi^{(T)}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{G},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left|\tilde{F}_{\delta}\left(\alpha,\xi^{(T)}\right)-G_{\delta}(\alpha)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{G},

where
\tilde{F}_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})
is the value computed from the

\varepsilon_{x}
-accurate inner solutions.

Proof.

Write
\xi^{\star}\triangleq\xi^{\star}(\alpha)
,
\mu\triangleq\mu_{\delta}
,

\mathsf{L}\triangleq\mathsf{L}_{\delta}
, and let

e_{t}\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\mathbf{h}(\tilde{g}_{i}^{(t)})-\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(t)})
,
so
\left\|e_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq b(\varepsilon_{x})\leq\mu r/4
by
Lemma
S2.42
(ii) and the choice of
\varepsilon_{x}
. Since

\xi^{\star}
maximizes the concave
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\cdot)
over

\mathbb{B}_{2}
, it is a fixed point:

\xi^{\star}=\Pi_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}(\xi^{\star}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{\star}))
. By nonexpansiveness
of
\Pi_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}
,

\left\|\xi^{(t+1)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left(\xi^{(t)}+\tfrac{1}{\mathsf{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(t)})\right)-\left(\xi^{\star}+\tfrac{1}{\mathsf{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{\star})\right)\right\|_{2}+\frac{\left\|e_{t}\right\|_{2}}{\mathsf{L}}.

By Lemma
S2.37
(ii,iv),
F_{\delta}(\alpha,\cdot)
is

C^{2}
on a neighborhood of the segment
[\xi^{(t)},\xi^{\star}]\subseteq\mathbb{B}_{2}
with

\mu I\preccurlyeq-\mathcal{D}_{\xi}^{2}F_{\delta}\preccurlyeq\mathsf{L}I
there, so with

\bar{H}\triangleq-\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{D}^{2}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{\star}+t(\xi^{(t)}-\xi^{\star}))\mathrm{d}t
we get

\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(t)})-\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{\star})=-\bar{H}(\xi^{(t)}-\xi^{\star})
and

\left\|\left(I-\tfrac{1}{\mathsf{L}}\bar{H}\right)\left(\xi^{(t)}-\xi^{\star}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{\mu}{\mathsf{L}}\right)\left\|\xi^{(t)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}.

Setting
q\triangleq 1-\mu/\mathsf{L}=1-\frac{1}{30\varkappa_{n}^{3/2}}
and iterating,

\left\|\xi^{(T)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}\leq q^{T}\left\|\xi^{(0)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\frac{b(\varepsilon_{x})}{\mathsf{L}}\sum_{j\geq 0}q^{j}\leq q^{T}+\frac{b(\varepsilon_{x})}{\mu}\leq\frac{r}{2}+\frac{r}{4}<r,

using
\left\|\xi^{(0)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}\leq 1
and

q^{T}\leq e^{-T\mu/\mathsf{L}}\leq r/2
by the choice of
T
. By

\mathsf{L}
-smoothness and
\left\|\mathcal{D}_{\xi}F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{\star})\right\|_{2}\leq\mathsf{M}_{\delta}
(Lemma
S2.37
(iv)),

G_{\delta}(\alpha)-F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})\leq\mathsf{M}_{\delta}\left\|\xi^{(T)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\frac{\mathsf{L}}{2}\left\|\xi^{(T)}-\xi^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(\mathsf{M}_{\delta}+\mathsf{L}_{\delta}\right)\,r=\varepsilon_{G},

using
r\leq 1
. Finally, Lemma
S2.42
(ii) gives

F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})\leq\tilde{F}_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})\leq F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})+\varepsilon_{x}\leq G_{\delta}(\alpha)+\varepsilon_{G}
, and

\tilde{F}_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})\geq F_{\delta}(\alpha,\xi^{(T)})\geq G_{\delta}(\alpha)-\varepsilon_{G}
.
∎

Lemma S2.44

(Trisection with inexact values)
.

Let
f
be concave on
[a,b]
and
\Lambda
-Lipschitz, and suppose an oracle
returns
\tilde{f}
with
\left|\tilde{f}-f\right|\leq\varepsilon_{G}
at any query point.
Run
k
rounds of trisection: given the current bracket, query the two
interior points
m_{1},m_{2}
trisecting it, discard the right third if

\tilde{f}(m_{1})\geq\tilde{f}(m_{2})
and the left third otherwise. Let

\hat{\alpha}
be the queried point with the largest
\tilde{f}
-value over all
rounds. Then

\displaystyle f(\hat{\alpha})\geq\max_{[a,b]}f-(2k+2)\,\varepsilon_{G}-\Lambda\,(b-a)\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{k},\hskip 16.38895pt\left|\tilde{f}(\hat{\alpha})-\max_{[a,b]}f\right|\leq(2k+3)\,\varepsilon_{G}+\Lambda(b-a)\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{k}.

(S.101)

Proof.

We claim each discard lowers the bracket maximum by at most
2\varepsilon_{G}
.
Consider a round with bracket
[u,v]
,
w\triangleq v-u
, interior points

m_{1}=u+w/3<m_{2}=v-w/3
, and suppose
\tilde{f}(m_{1})\geq\tilde{f}(m_{2})
, so

(m_{2},v]
is discarded and
f(m_{1})\geq f(m_{2})-2\varepsilon_{G}
. Let

x^{\star}\in\argmax_{[u,v]}f
. If
x^{\star}\leq m_{2}
nothing is lost.
Otherwise
x^{\star}\in(m_{2},v]
and

m_{2}=\lambda m_{1}+(1-\lambda)x^{\star}
with

\lambda=\frac{x^{\star}-m_{2}}{x^{\star}-m_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, because

x^{\star}-m_{2}\leq w/3
and
x^{\star}-m_{1}\geq w/3
; concavity gives

f(m_{2})\geq\lambda f(m_{1})+(1-\lambda)f(x^{\star})\geq\lambda\left(f(m_{2})-2\varepsilon_{G}\right)+(1-\lambda)f(x^{\star})
, hence

f(m_{2})\geq f(x^{\star})-2\varepsilon_{G}\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\geq f(x^{\star})-2\varepsilon_{G}
, and
m_{2}
remains in the new bracket. The
discarded-left case is symmetric. By induction, after
k
rounds the
bracket
B_{k}
has width
(b-a)(2/3)^{k}
and

\max_{B_{k}}f\geq\max_{[a,b]}f-2k\varepsilon_{G}
. Both round-
k
query points lie in

B_{k}
, so one of them, say
m
, satisfies

f(m)\geq\max_{B_{k}}f-\Lambda(b-a)(2/3)^{k}
by the Lipschitz property. Then

f(\hat{\alpha})\geq\tilde{f}(\hat{\alpha})-\varepsilon_{G}\geq\tilde{f}(m)-\varepsilon_{G}\geq f(m)-2\varepsilon_{G}
, which proves the first claim; the second follows from

\left|\tilde{f}(\hat{\alpha})-f(\hat{\alpha})\right|\leq\varepsilon_{G}
and

f(\hat{\alpha})\leq\max_{[a,b]}f
.
∎

The certified nested scheme is summarized in
Algorithm
1
; its parameter choices implement
Lemmas
S2.42
–
S2.44
, and its guarantees
are those of Theorem 4.3.

Algorithm 1

Certified nested scheme for the computable WP test

\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{n}

0:

data
(X_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, level
\varrho
, accuracy
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}
,
curvature bound
K_{\mathrm{curv}}
from (21).

1:

Compute
\mathcal{V}_{n}
,
\ell_{n}
,
U_{n}
,
\varkappa_{n}
,

\hat{z}\triangleq\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}
from (9),

\delta\triangleq\hat{z}/n
, and the constants of (24)
and (
S.91
).

2:

if
the observable condition
\mathfrak{E}_{n}

of (30) fails
then

3:

return
“fail to reject
\mathcal{H}_{0}
”.

4:

end

if

5:

Set

k^{\star}\triangleq\max\left\{1,\big\lceil\log_{3/2}\left(6\Lambda_{\delta}\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}\big/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}\right)\big\rceil\right\}
,

\varepsilon_{G}\triangleq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}/(3(k^{\star}+2))
,

r\triangleq\varepsilon_{G}/(\mathsf{M}_{\delta}+\mathsf{L}_{\delta})
,

T\triangleq\big\lceil 30\varkappa_{n}^{3/2}\ln(2/r)\big\rceil
,

\varepsilon_{x}\triangleq\min\left\{\varepsilon_{G},\,\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\delta/\ell_{n}},\,\left(\mu_{\delta}r/(16\mathsf{s}_{n})\right)^{2}\sqrt{\ell_{n}/\delta}\right\}
, and

k_{\mathrm{in}}\triangleq\big\lceil\log_{5/2}\left(2\mathsf{s}_{n}^{2}\sqrt{U_{n}\delta}/(\ell_{n}\varepsilon_{x})\right)\big\rceil
.

6:

[a,b]\leftarrow[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]
;
\hat{\Phi}_{n}\leftarrow-\infty
.

7:

for

k=1,\ldots,k^{\star}

do

8:

m_{1}\leftarrow a+(b-a)/3
;
m_{2}\leftarrow b-(b-a)/3
.

9:

for

\alpha\in\{m_{1},m_{2}\}

do

10:

\xi\leftarrow\mathbf{0}
.

11:

for

t=1,\ldots,T

do

12:

For each
i\in[n]
in parallel: starting from

x\leftarrow X_{i}
, run
k_{\mathrm{in}}
steps

x\leftarrow x-\frac{1}{2\alpha+K_{\mathrm{curv}}}\Sigma\nabla\psi_{i}(x)
for

\psi_{i}(x)=\xi^{\top}\mathbf{h}(x)+\alpha\left\|x-X_{i}\right\|_{\Sigma}^{2}
, and set

\tilde{g}_{i}\leftarrow x
.

13:

\xi\leftarrow\Pi_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\left(\xi+\frac{1}{\mathsf{L}_{\delta}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{h}(\tilde{g}_{i})\right)
.

14:

end

for

15:

Recompute
(\tilde{g}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
at the current

(\alpha,\xi)
as above, and set

\tilde{G}(\alpha)\leftarrow-\alpha\delta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(\tilde{g}_{i})
.

16:

\hat{\Phi}_{n}\leftarrow\max\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{n},\tilde{G}(\alpha)\right\}
.

17:

end

for

18:

if

\tilde{G}(m_{1})\geq\tilde{G}(m_{2})

then

19:

b\leftarrow m_{2}

20:

else

21:

a\leftarrow m_{1}

22:

end

if

23:

end

for

23:

reject
\mathcal{H}_{0}
if and only if
\hat{\Phi}_{n}>\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}
.

S2.10.5
Statistical inheritance lemmas

The next lemma isolates the two inputs from the coverage analysis of the
paper that the inheritance argument needs; its proof consists of precise
pointers into the corresponding existing proofs.

Lemma S2.45

(Edgeworth-expansion inputs from the coverage proofs)
.

The following hold.

(i)

Under Assumptions 2.4–2.10, there
exist an event
\mathcal{G}_{n}
with
\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{c})=O(1/n)
, a constant

C<\infty
, and a deterministic sequence
\delta_{n}=\widetilde{O}(1/n)
such
that, on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,

\displaystyle nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\right)\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)+\varepsilon_{n}^{\Sigma},

(S.102)

where
\gamma^{\dagger}
is the random vector defined
in (
S.45
),

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\triangleq\mathcal{L}_{W,V}(\mathcal{W}_{n}-W,\mathcal{V}_{n}-V)
satisfies

\left|\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
n
large,

\left|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|\leq C\log^{4}(n)/n
, and

\left|\varepsilon_{n}^{\Sigma}\right|\leq\delta_{n}
. Moreover, there are distribution
functions
\Psi_{n}^{\dagger}
with

\sup_{z}\left|\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq z)-\Psi_{n}^{\dagger}(z)\right|=\widetilde{O}(1/n)
, whose densities are bounded on the
interval
[z_{1-\varrho}/2,\,2z_{1-\varrho}]
by a constant

C_{\Psi}^{\prime}<\infty
uniformly in
n
.

(ii)

(
d_{h}=1
.)
Under the assumptions of
Theorem 3.10, there exist an event of probability

1-O(n^{-3/2})
, constants
0<c_{1}\leq c_{2}<\infty
, and a deterministic
sequence
\hat{\delta}_{n}=\widetilde{O}(n^{-3/2})
such that, on that event,

\displaystyle nR_{n}(h)-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{\mathcal{W}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\left(\hat{\gamma}^{2}-\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\hat{\varepsilon}_{n},\qquad c_{1}\leq\frac{\mathcal{W}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\leq c_{2},\qquad\left|\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}\right|\leq\hat{\delta}_{n},

(S.103)

where
\hat{\gamma}
is the signed-root statistic constructed in the proof
of Theorem 3.11, which admits an Edgeworth expansion
valid to order
n^{-3/2}
whose distribution functions have densities
uniformly bounded on compact subsets of
\mathds{R}
.

Proof.

(i)
The representation is established in the proof of
Theorem 3.4: it is
display (
S.47
) there, with

\varepsilon_{n}^{\Sigma}\triangleq\bar{\varepsilon}_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}
(in the notation of that
proof) controlled
by (
S.40
),
\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}
controlled by (
S.46
),

\gamma^{\dagger}
defined in (
S.45
), and

\left|\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}
for
n
large by (
S.43
); the event

\mathcal{G}_{n}
is the intersection of the probability-
1-O(1/n)
events on which
those displays hold. The validity of the Edgeworth expansion of

\gamma^{\dagger}
up to order
n^{-1}
is established in the closing
paragraph of that proof (via the moment expansion (
S.31
)
and Lemma
S2.4
); the leading term of
\Psi_{n}^{\dagger}
is the
distribution function
\Psi
of Theorem 3.2, which is
continuously differentiable on
(0,\infty)
, and the
n^{-1/2}
Edgeworth
correction is a polynomial multiple of a Gaussian density, so the densities
are uniformly bounded on the stated compact interval.

(ii)
The representation is display (
S.65
) in the
proof of Theorem 3.11(Section
S2.9.10
), together with the bound

\left|\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}\right|\leq\hat{\delta}_{n}
stated there, and

\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=(\mathcal{W}_{n}/\mathcal{V}_{n})\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
for
d_{h}=1
. The
two-sided bound on
\mathcal{W}_{n}/\mathcal{V}_{n}
at the stated probability level follows
from the concentration bounds recorded in the proof of
Proposition 3.13 (under Assumption 3.9, both

\mathcal{W}_{n}
and
\mathcal{V}_{n}
deviate from the positive constants
W
and
V
by

\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{\log(n)/n})
with probability
1-O(n^{-3/2})
). The
Edgeworth expansion of
\hat{\gamma}
valid to order
n^{-3/2}
is
established in the same proof via
[
4
, Theorem 2]

(with Theorem 3.10 supplying the expansion of

nR_{n}(h)
); its terms are a Gaussian distribution function plus polynomial
multiples of the Gaussian density, whose densities are bounded on compacts.
∎

Lemma S2.46

(Crossing-band probability)
.

The following hold.

(i)

Under Assumptions 2.4–2.10 (the
hypotheses of Theorem 3.4),
there is a constant
C_{\Psi}<\infty
such that for every deterministic
sequence
w_{n}\in[0,z_{1-\varrho}/4]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(0<nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq w_{n}\right)\leq C_{\Psi}\,w_{n}+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(S.104)

(ii)

(
d_{h}=1
, Bartlett radius.)
Under the assumptions of
Theorem 3.10, with
C=O(1/n)
as in
Proposition 3.12, there is a constant

C_{\Psi}^{\flat}<\infty
such that for every deterministic sequence

w_{n}\in[0,\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}/4]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(0<nR_{n}(h)-(1-C)\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq w_{n}\right)\leq C_{\Psi}^{\flat}\,w_{n}+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-3/2}\right).

(S.105)

Proof.

(i)
Let
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,
\gamma^{\dagger}
,
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}
,

\varepsilon_{n}^{\dagger}
,
\varepsilon_{n}^{\Sigma}
,
\delta_{n}
, and
\Psi_{n}^{\dagger}
be as in
Lemma
S2.45
(i). On
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, the event

\left\{0<nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq w_{n}\right\}

and (
S.102
) force

-\delta_{n}<\left(1-\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\right)\left(\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}+\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right)\leq w_{n}+\delta_{n},

and since
\frac{1}{2}\leq 1-\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\circ}\leq\frac{3}{2}
this implies

\left\|\gamma^{\dagger}\right\|_{2}^{2}-z_{1-\varrho}\in\big(-2\delta_{n}-\left|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|,\,2(w_{n}+\delta_{n})+\left|\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{n}\right|\big]
, an interval whose endpoints lie in

[z_{1-\varrho}/2,\,2z_{1-\varrho}]-z_{1-\varrho}
for
n
large because

w_{n}\leq z_{1-\varrho}/4
. Hence, by the Edgeworth approximation and the
density bound of Lemma
S2.45
(i),

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(0<nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq w_{n}\right)\leq C_{\Psi}^{\prime}\left(2w_{n}+4\delta_{n}+2C\frac{\log^{4}n}{n}\right)+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{c})=C_{\Psi}\,w_{n}+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(ii)
Work on the event of
Lemma
S2.45
(ii). Since

\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=(\mathcal{W}_{n}/\mathcal{V}_{n})\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}
,
representation (
S.103
) gives

nR_{n}(h)-(1-C)\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}=\frac{\mathcal{W}_{n}}{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\left(\hat{\gamma}^{2}-(1-C)\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\right)+\hat{\varepsilon}_{n}.

On the band, using
c_{1}\leq\mathcal{W}_{n}/\mathcal{V}_{n}\leq c_{2}
,

\hat{\gamma}^{2}-(1-C)\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}\in\Big(-\frac{\hat{\delta}_{n}}{c_{1}},\ \frac{w_{n}+\hat{\delta}_{n}}{c_{1}}\Big].

Since
C=O(1/n)
and
w_{n}\leq\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}/4
, the resulting
interval
(a_{n},b_{n}]
for
\hat{\gamma}^{2}
satisfies

a_{n}\geq\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}/2
for
n
large and

b_{n}-a_{n}\leq(w_{n}+2\hat{\delta}_{n})/c_{1}
. Writing

\mathbb{P}^{\star}(a_{n}<\hat{\gamma}^{2}\leq b_{n})=\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\sqrt{a_{n}}<\hat{\gamma}\leq\sqrt{b_{n}})+\mathbb{P}^{\star}(-\sqrt{b_{n}}\leq\hat{\gamma}<-\sqrt{a_{n}})
and applying the
Edgeworth expansion of
\hat{\gamma}
(valid to order
n^{-3/2}
, with
densities bounded on compacts) on each interval of length

\sqrt{b_{n}}-\sqrt{a_{n}}\leq(b_{n}-a_{n})/(2\sqrt{a_{n}})
yields, enlarging the
generic constant
C^{\prime}
if necessary and absorbing the probability of the
complement of the working event into the
\widetilde{O}(n^{-3/2})
term,

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\left(0<nR_{n}(h)-(1-C)\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}\leq w_{n}\right)\leq C^{\prime}\,\frac{w_{n}+2\hat{\delta}_{n}}{c_{1}\sqrt{2\chi^{2}_{1;1-\varrho}}}+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-3/2}\right)=C_{\Psi}^{\flat}\,w_{n}+\widetilde{O}\left(n^{-3/2}\right).

∎

S2.10.6
Proof of Theorem 4.3

Proof.

Part (i) and the second inclusion in part (ii) are deterministic statements on

\mathfrak{E}_{n}
. The first inclusion in part (ii) holds globally because the
computable test fails to reject off
\mathfrak{E}_{n}
. Part (iii) combines a
deterministic work bound with a high-probability event controlling the empirical
condition number and data scales.

The total work count used in part (iii), measured in first-order oracle calls for
\mathbf{h}
, is

\underbrace{2k^{\star}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{trisection}\\
\text{queries}\end{subarray}}\times\underbrace{(T+1)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{PGA steps and value}\\
\text{evaluation per query}\end{subarray}}\times\underbrace{n}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{inner problems}\\
\text{per step}\end{subarray}}\times\underbrace{(k_{\mathrm{in}}+1)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{GD steps and output evaluation}\\
\text{per inner problem}\end{subarray}}.

(S.106)

(i)
On
\mathfrak{E}_{n}
we have
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
, so
Lemma
S2.37
(v) makes
G_{\delta}
concave and

\Lambda_{\delta}
-Lipschitz on

[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]
, an interval of length at
most
\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta}
by (B1); each query is
\varepsilon_{G}
-accurate by
Lemma
S2.43
, whose admissibility condition

\varepsilon_{G}\leq\mathsf{M}_{\delta}+\mathsf{L}_{\delta}
holds on
\mathfrak{E}_{n}

since
\varepsilon_{G}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}\leq\tfrac{1}{8}\sqrt{\ell_{n}\delta}\leq\mathsf{L}_{\delta}
; and

\max_{[\underline{\alpha}_{\delta},\overline{\alpha}_{\delta}]}G_{\delta}=\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)
.
Lemma
S2.44
with
k=k^{\star}
then gives

\displaystyle\Big|\hat{\Phi}_{n}-\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq(2k^{\star}+3)\,\varepsilon_{G}+\Lambda_{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{U_{n}}{\delta}}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2k^{\star}+3}{3(k^{\star}+2)}\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}}{6}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}+\frac{1}{6}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n},

where
(2/3)^{k^{\star}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}/(6\Lambda_{\delta}\sqrt{U_{n}/\delta})
by the
choice of
k^{\star}
.

(ii)
First inclusion: off
\mathfrak{E}_{n}
the computable test fails to
reject. On
\mathfrak{E}_{n}
, if
\hat{\Phi}_{n}>\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}
, then by (i)

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)>0
, so
Proposition 4.2(i)
(display (27)) gives
R_{n}(\mathbf{h})>\delta
, i.e.

nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})>\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}
. Second inclusion: on
\mathfrak{E}_{n}
, suppose

nR_{n}(\mathbf{h})>\hat{z}_{1-\varrho}+w_{n}
, i.e.

R_{n}(\mathbf{h})-\delta>w_{n}/n=5\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}\sqrt{2\delta/\ell_{n}}
. By the
contrapositive of Corollary
S2.41
(applicable on

\mathfrak{E}_{n}
),
\Phi_{n}(\delta)>2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}
; by
Proposition 4.2(i),

\Phi^{\mathrm{loc}}_{n}(\delta)=\Phi_{n}(\delta)
, and by (i),

\hat{\Phi}_{n}\geq\Phi_{n}(\delta)-\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}>\varepsilon_{\mathrm{alg},n}
: the test rejects.

(iii)
The count (
S.106
) is immediate from the
construction:
